* DONE [#D] vulcan re-declares nix-config's inputs by hand; mirror upstream's inputSet merge instead
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-czc
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: task
:CREATED:  [2026-08-15 Sat 11:32]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-15 Sat 23:15]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-15 Sat 23:15]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
/etc/nixos consumes nix-config with `flake = false`, so nix-config's own outputs
function never runs. That function is where upstream assembles the input set its
home-manager modules actually read:

    portableInputs = rootInputs.nix-config-ai.lib.inputSet;   # nix-config/flake.nix:141
    inputs         = rootInputs // portableInputs;            # nix-config/flake.nix:142

and config/ai/flake.nix builds that set as
`builtins.removeAttrs inputs ["self"]`, exporting it as `lib.inputSet`.

Because vulcan skips that merge, every input nix-config's modules reference must
be declared by hand here. This has now bitten twice:

  - obr          -- declared after the first breakage
  - rust-overlay -- declared 2026-08-15 in 779f43fc3, after the whole system
                    became unbuildable for ~5 hours

Each time the symptom is identical and total: an assertion in config/obr.nix
aborts system.build.toplevel, so NOTHING rebuilds, and the cause is invisible
from this repo -- nothing here changed, an input moved.

PROPOSAL: mirror upstream's merge instead of chasing inputs individually.
`nix-config-ai` is ALREADY a root input here (verified: root.inputs.nix-config-ai
is present), so `inputs.nix-config-ai.lib.inputSet` is reachable today with no
new input. The change would be in modules/users/home-manager/default.nix:30,
which currently passes the raw set:

    extraSpecialArgs = { hostname = ...; inherit inputs; };

DIRECTION MATTERS, and this is the part needing a decision:

  a) inputs // inputs.nix-config-ai.lib.inputSet
     Exactly what upstream does. But it lets nix-config-ai's inputs SHADOW
     vulcan's -- including `nixpkgs`, where nix-config-ai tracks
     nixpkgs-unstable while this host is pinned to 25.11. Any module reading
     inputs.nixpkgs would silently switch channels. Upstream can accept this
     because its rootInputs are designed around it; vulcan's are not.

  b) inputs.nix-config-ai.lib.inputSet // inputs
     Portable inputs fill gaps only; vulcan's own always win. Fixes the class
     without the shadowing hazard. Diverges from upstream's ordering, so a
     module that genuinely WANTS the portable version of a shared input would
     not get it.

(b) looks right for this host, but it is a real choice about how vulcan consumes
nix-config and it touches the input set of every home-manager module, so it
wants a decision rather than an autonomous edit.

Worth noting either way: the two known inputs are now declared and the system
builds, so this is prevention, not an outage.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

IMPLEMENTED in the direction John chose: inputSet // inputs (gaps only, vulcan wins collisions).

modules/users/home-manager/default.nix now passes
  inputs = inputs.nix-config-ai.lib.inputSet // inputs;
to extraSpecialArgs, mirroring what nix-config itself does for consumers that
take it as a real flake (rootInputs // portableInputs) -- which vulcan does not
get, because it consumes nix-config with flake = false so that outputs function
never runs.

DIRECTION VERIFIED, not assumed. Evaluated the merged set live:
  merged has rust-overlay = YES   (in the portable set, not a vulcan root input)
  merged has git-ai       = YES
  merged.nixpkgs == vulcan nixpkgs = YES
  35 keys total
That last line is the point of inverting upstream's ordering: nix-config-ai's
inputSet carries its own nixpkgs tracking nixpkgs-unstable, and upstream's
rootInputs // portableInputs would let it shadow this host's 25.11 pin. With
inputSet // inputs the portable entries only fill gaps.

Built and switched: the configuration evaluates and the running system is
2c0n24wgzcq5fy0ksjwgsfscyh1bnbbc, confirmed by nix derivation show to be the
realisation of the committed tree. 0 failed units afterwards.

Scope note recorded earlier on this issue and unchanged: this is PREVENTION, not
repair. Measured exposure is that only 'obr' carries an assertion in the shared
config and it was already declared, so nothing was broken at the time of the fix.
What it buys is that the next input nix-config starts requiring arrives
automatically rather than taking the host down first -- which is exactly how
rust-overlay behaved on 2026-08-15 (nixos-sil).

** Comments

#+begin_src json
[
  {
    "id": 4,
    "issue_id": "nixos-czc",
    "author": "johnw",
    "text": "2026-08-15, later the same day -- MEASURED THE ACTUAL EXPOSURE. The acute instance\nis gone and the residual risk is much smaller than this issue implies, so the\nproposal below should be judged on prevention, not on outage risk.\n\nWhat changed: John advanced nix-config to 1e0d2f6e (\"simplified Obr integration\",\nconsumer cd3bd2830) and removed the rust-overlay declaration I had added. Verified\nthis cycle -- flake.nix contains 0 occurrences of rust-overlay, it is no longer a\nroot input, and the tree evaluates to a derivation that realises to exactly the\nrunning /run/current-system.\n\nThe new config/obr.nix no longer builds obr from source with a Rust toolchain; it\ntakes inputs.obr.packages.<system>.default directly. That is what removed the\nrust-overlay requirement at the root.\n\nEXPOSURE NOW, measured rather than assumed. Vulcan imports exactly two files from\nnix-config:\n\n  config/packages.nix -- references inputs.llm-agents and inputs.nix-config-ai.\n                         BOTH declared here. No exposure.\n  config/johnw.nix    -- references inputs.git-ai, and pulls in config/obr.nix.\n\nPer-input status across nix-config's config/ tree:\n\n  obr        3 refs, 1 guard, 1 ASSERTION  -- the ONLY mandatory input. Declared here.\n  git-ai     2 refs, 3 guards              -- fully guarded by (inputs ? git-ai),\n                                              so vulcan silently does not get its\n                                              home-manager module or package. A\n                                              feature gap, not a break.\n  dirscan, emacs-src, hours, ledger, org2tc, scripts, stock-trader, git-scripts\n             1 unguarded ref each, ALL in config/overlays.nix -- which vulcan does\n             NOT import (confirmed: the only two ${inputs.nix-config}/... paths in\n             this repo are packages.nix and johnw.nix).\n\nSo there is NO current exposure. Correcting my own earlier reading in the process:\nI first flagged git-ai as unguarded in johnw.nix, which was a grep artifact -- my\nguard pattern used \"inputs ? \" with a trailing space and missed the parenthesised\n\"(inputs ? git-ai)\" form. It is guarded three times over.\n\nTHE RISK THAT REMAINS is therefore narrower than originally written:\n\n  1. A NEW asserted input upstream breaks every rebuild here, exactly as\n     rust-overlay did. Today only obr asserts, and it is declared. This is the\n     real recurrence risk and the reason to keep this issue open.\n  2. Importing config/overlays.nix would break on 8 unguarded references at once.\n     Nothing plans to, but it is a sharp edge worth knowing about.\n  3. Optional inputs degrade SILENTLY. Vulcan is missing git-ai today and nothing\n     reports that. If it should have it, that is a separate decision.\n\nThe proposal below is unchanged and still John's call, including merge direction:\ninputs // inputSet matches upstream but lets nix-config-ai's nixpkgs-unstable\nshadow this host's 25.11, while inputSet // inputs fills gaps only and keeps\nvulcan's own inputs winning. Given (1) is now the only outage path and it is\ncurrently closed, this is prevention rather than repair -- so a lower priority\nthan when it was filed is defensible.\n",

* DONE [#C] git-annex unusable in /tank/Archives as johnw: chmod needs ownership, and crawl output arrives zimit-owned
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-dhm
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 17:29]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-20 Thu 17:29]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-20 Thu 17:29]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fixed and verified end to end; full analysis in the description. ga fsck now reports 25 ok / 0 failures, new crawl output is handed to johnw automatically, and the tmpfiles declaration matches reality so a future switch cannot silently revert it.
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
`ga fsck` in /tank/Archives failed on 8 of 25 files with
"setFileMode: permission denied (Operation not permitted)", plus a trailing
"SQLite3 returned ErrorReadOnly ... attempt to write a readonly database".

ROOT CAUSE. The error is EPERM ("Operation not permitted"), NOT EACCES
("Permission denied") -- different failures, and the distinction is the whole
diagnosis. chmod() requires OWNERSHIP of the file. Group write is irrelevant: the
kernel returns EPERM to a non-owner however permissive the mode. Proven in a
controlled test on a zimit:johnw 664 file:
    as johnw, write  -> succeeded (group write works)
    as johnw, chmod  -> rc=1, "Operation not permitted"
    as johnw, chmod on a file he OWNS -> rc=0   (so the test discriminates)
The tree is zimit:johnw throughout, so johnw can read and write every object but
can chmod none of them.

WHY fsck NEEDED TO CHMOD AT ALL -- the precise trigger, narrower than "wrong
owner". git-annex keeps objects read-only (444, dirs 555). The earlier ownership
work left them at 775, so fsck tried to REPAIR the mode, and repair needs
ownership. Confirmed decisively: setting ONE object back to 444 and its directory
to 555, changing no ownership at all, made fsck pass on that file immediately.
Only 24 files and 8 directories were affected, not the whole 390G annex.

The sqlite error is the same cause downstream: git-annex could not thaw its own
fsck database.

WHAT DID NOT WORK, recorded so it is not retried: annex.freezecontent-command and
annex.thawcontent-command set to no-ops. git-annex 10.20251114 still calls
setFileMode directly on the fsck repair path, and the failure was identical. Both
settings were reverted. annex.crippledfilesystem was NOT tried -- it converts the
repo to unlocked mode, which is a large and risky change to a 390G locked annex.

FIXED IN THREE PARTS:
 1. Mode repair (immediate). Restored 444/555 across .git/annex/objects. `ga fsck`
    now reports 25 files ok, 0 failures, no sqlite error.
 2. Handoff chown (durable). The runner does `mv "$zim" "$ZIM_DIR/"` as zimit, so
    every NEW archive arrived owned by zimit -- and johnw cannot annex those at
    all. Verified in a throwaway repo: `git annex add` on a zimit-owned file
    rc=1, on a johnw-owned file rc=0. Added a root-prefixed ExecStopPost that
    chowns real (non-symlink) *.zim in ZIM_DIR to johnw:zimit 644. Group stays
    zimit and mode 644 because kiwix-serve runs as zimit and must still read them
    -- confirmed johnw:zimit 644 is readable by zimit while 640 is not, so the
    group bit is doing real work. Symlinks are skipped so already-annexed entries
    are untouched. It runs on every invocation including no-op polls, so a missed
    handoff self-heals.
 3. tmpfiles alignment. The rule declared `d ZIM 0755 zimit zimit` while the live
    directory was 0775 zimit:johnw -- a hand-applied fix the declaration would
    revert on any switch where tmpfiles re-applied ownership, silently removing
    the group write johnw needs to create annex symlinks. Now declares
    0775 zimit johnw, matching reality.

VERIFIED END TO END: handoff test file went zimit:zimit 644 -> johnw:zimit 644,
an existing annexed symlink was left untouched, johnw then annexed the test file
successfully, and the test artefacts were dropped and removed leaving `git status`
clean and 0 objects with a wrong mode. After the final switch the tmpfiles rule
and reality agree, both johnw and zimit can write ZIM_DIR, and `ga fsck` reports
25 ok / 0 failures.

WHY NOT SIMPLY CHOWN EVERYTHING TO johnw: that was tried before this issue and
broke Zimit, because ZIM_DIR itself became johnw-owned and zimit -- which is not
in group johnw -- lost the write access it needs to `mv` finished crawls in. The
fix above keeps zimit owning the directory and moves only the FILES after they
land.
#+end_example

* DONE [#C] restartUnits races home-manager: pushme runs with stale config during a switch
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-djx
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 04:46]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-12 Wed 10:02]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 10:02]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fixed by removing restartUnits from the sops secret (commit 7e93bf16b). It caused two failures: (1) the stale-config race this issue describes — sops-nix restarts the unit in a separate systemd transaction, so 'after = home-manager-johnw.service' does not constrain it, and at 00:21:26 the sync ran two seconds before the switch finished using the old fileset; (2) worse, it BLOCKED EVERY SWITCH — the unit is Type=oneshot so systemctl start blocks until completion, and with TimeoutStartSec at 4h a switch could hang four hours holding the build lock. Observed live: a switch sat >8 minutes mid-sync until the sync was stopped by hand. Chose option (a) from the issue: drop restartUnits and accept that a rotated key is picked up at the next hourly run. Verified: no sops restart references remain, and a switch now completes and releases the lock while the sync runs.
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

On the 2026-08-12 00:21 switch, pushme-positron.service ran at 00:21:26 and failed, two seconds BEFORE the switch finished at 00:21:28. It used the OLD fileset (destination /tank/work/positron) and still performed the test -d probe, even though the new module sets a new path and CheckRemoteDirectory: false — because home-manager had not yet re-linked ~/.config/pushme/*.

Trigger is sops.secrets."pushme/positron-ssh-private-key".restartUnits = [ "pushme-positron.service" ]. sops-nix restarts the listed unit when the secret is (re)deployed, and that restart is a SEPARATE systemd transaction, so the unit's 'after = home-manager-johnw.service' ordering does not constrain it. The same race produced the very first failure of this unit on 2026-08-10 23:03:39.

Consequences: a sync can run against stale configuration at every switch — wrong destination, wrong flags. Here it merely failed; with a valid-but-stale destination it would silently write to the wrong place, and rsync carries --delete.

Options to consider (none applied yet):
  a) drop restartUnits and accept that a rotated key is picked up at the next hourly run
  b) add ConditionPathExists or a guard in the unit that verifies the config is the expected generation before running
  c) have the unit read its config from the Nix store rather than ~/.config, removing home-manager from the runtime path entirely — the same fix used for the Loki rules and vdirsyncer (see nixos-ye9)

Option (c) is the structural one and matches how two other silent failures on this host were resolved.

* DONE [#E] In-VM hang detector probes /v1/capabilities with an invalid API key, 4300 warnings/day
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-dnx
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-26 Wed 16:01]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-27 Thu 16:01]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-27 Thu 16:01]
:SOURCE_REPO: .
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
PRE-EXISTING, NOT URGENT, and it needs John because the fix involves an API key.

FOUND 2026-08-26 while confirming Hermes health after the self-heal fixes. hermes/.hermes/logs/errors.log carries a WARNING every ~20 seconds:
  gateway.platforms.api_server: API server rejected invalid API key: remote='127.0.0.1' peer_ip='127.0.0.1' method='GET' path='/v1/capabilities' user_agent='cur...'

VOLUME: ~4,300/day, sustained. 10,069 in the current errors.log (first 2026-08-24 14:46) and 10,479 in errors.log.1 covering 08-22..08-24, so it has been running at that rate for at least five days and predates every change made today.

WHO: the caller is inside the VM, uses curl, and hits /v1/capabilities on a 20s cadence. That matches hermes-hang-capture in modules/services/hermes-vm.nix:1316 -- 'Probes the local api_server every 20s; after 3 consecutive non-responses ... writes per-thread scheduler state + kernel stacks'. The host-side hermes-health-check is NOT the culprit: it is Python, not curl, and it succeeds (hermes_api_server_ok = 1).

SEVERITY IS LOW, and the reason is worth stating rather than assuming. The detector's job is liveness, and a 401 still proves the HTTP server answered -- a genuinely frozen gateway would not respond at all. Checked rather than inferred: /var/lib/hermes/.hermes/diag/ holds 6 captures whose newest is 2026-07-03, so the detector is NOT misfiring and has not been triggered by these 401s. The safety net still works.

WHAT IT ACTUALLY COSTS:
 - ~4,300 spurious WARNING lines/day, which is most of errors.log (2 MB and rotating).
 - Real authentication failures would be indistinguishable in that noise.
 - The probe does not verify what it appears to verify: it is credentialed in form but not in fact, so nobody would notice if the key handling regressed further.

FIX (John's call, since it touches a key): give the hang detector the API key the api_server expects, or -- if liveness is genuinely all it needs -- point it at an unauthenticated endpoint or have it accept 401 explicitly and stop sending a credential it does not have. The second option is arguably cleaner: it makes the probe's intent honest instead of leaving a permanently-failing credential in place.

NOTE: I did not attempt to read, compare or stage any key material.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

FIXED and verified 2026-08-27. Pointed the in-VM hang detector at /health instead of /v1/capabilities, via HANG_PROBE_URL in modules/services/hermes-vm.nix.

WHY THIS IS THE RIGHT FIX rather than adding a credential: the detector sends no API key BY DESIGN -- scripts/hermes-hang-capture.sh documents that any HTTP response, 401 included, proves the event loop is alive, 'which means we never need the API_SERVER_KEY here'. That reasoning is correct and unchanged. The problem was purely that the api_server logs every unauthenticated request at WARNING, so a 20-second anonymous probe wrote ~4,300 lines/day and dominated errors.log, where a genuine authentication failure would have been indistinguishable from the noise.

LIVENESS IS NOT WEAKENED. /health and /v1/capabilities are served by the same api_server on the same port in the same process, so a frozen event loop stops answering both. The detector's forensic capture path, thresholds and 20s cadence are untouched.

MEASURED BEFORE CHANGING ANYTHING, with a control:
  3 requests to /health           -> 0 new 'rejected invalid API key' lines
  3 requests to /v1/capabilities  -> exactly 3 new lines
The control matters: without it, zero could have meant the counter was broken rather than the endpoint being quiet.

VERIFIED AFTER DEPLOY: rejections were 3890 before the switch and 3899 immediately after (probes still running on the old config during activation), then 3899 again 90 seconds later -- 0 new in a window that previously produced 4-5. hermes_api_server_ok=1 and the Discord heartbeat present afterwards.

COST: the change is in the VM config, so the switch restarted the Hermes VM and paid the ~2m30s stop timeout tracked in nixos-m90.

** Comments

#+begin_src json
[
  {
    "id": 51,
    "issue_id": "nixos-dnx",
    "author": "johnw",
    "text": "CORRECTION 2026-08-26: I filed this on a wrong premise. The absent API key is DELIBERATE and documented, not a misconfiguration.\n\nscripts/hermes-hang-capture.sh states the design outright:\n  'Liveness model: ANY HTTP response (even 401 Unauthorized) proves the event loop is alive and accepting connections, so it counts as healthy. Only a timeout / connection refusal -- curl reports http_code 000 -- counts as a miss. That is exactly the signature of a frozen loop, and means we never need the API_SERVER_KEY here.'\n\nAnd the code matches the comment: the probe is a bare\n  curl -s -o /dev/null -m 5 -w '%{http_code}' \"$PROBE_URL\"\nwith no Authorization header at all, and the miss test is\n  if [ -n \"$code\" ] && [ \"$code\" != \"000\" ]  -> healthy\nSo a 401 resets the failure counter, exactly as intended. The detector sends no credential BY DESIGN because it only needs to know the event loop answers.\n\nWHAT I GOT WRONG: I described the probe as 'credentialed in form but not in fact', said it 'does not verify what it appears to verify', and concluded the fix was John's because it touches an API key. All three are incorrect. No key is needed, none is sent, and nothing about key handling is broken. I inferred a misconfiguration from the server-side log message ('rejected invalid API key') without reading the client that produces it.\n\nWHAT REMAINS TRUE, and is the whole of the issue: the api_server logs every unauthenticated request at WARNING, so a deliberately anonymous liveness probe every 20s produces ~4,300 WARNING lines/day and dominates errors.log. That is a SERVER-SIDE LOGGING concern, not a defect in the detector, and it does not need John or any secret.\n\nREVISED FIX OPTIONS (no key involved):\n a) have the api_server log unauthenticated /v1/capabilities requests at DEBUG, or rate-limit that warning;\n b) give the detector an unauthenticated health endpoint to probe instead;\n c) accept the noise and filter it where errors.log is consumed.\n(a) is upstream in the hermes-agent gateway, so (b) or (c) may be more practical here.\n\nRe-prioritised to P4: pure log hygiene, no functional impact. The detector works correctly and, per the diag directory, has not misfired since 2026-07-03.",
    "created_at": "2026-08-27T01:12:58Z"
  }
]
#+end_src

* DONE [#E] meta-monitoring.yaml header says two textfile cadence tiers; there are three
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-eb9
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: docs
:CREATED:  [2026-08-19 Wed 22:58]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-19 Wed 23:10]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-19 Wed 23:10]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fixed with the sweep: the header now names all three tiers (fast 30m, hourly 3h, daily 26h) plus TextfileCollectorScrapeError and the 2026-08-15 coverage backstop, and states why the count matters -- the wrong tier means a collector either flaps or is silently unwatched. Verified the file still loads and the build is clean.
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

modules/monitoring/alerts/meta-monitoring.yaml lines ~310-317 describe the textfile
freshness rules as split into TWO cadence groups (fast, daily), "verified live 2026-06-10".

There are now THREE tiers -- Fast 30m, Hourly 3h, Daily 26h -- plus
TextfileCollectorScrapeError and the 2026-08-15 coverage backstop.

Pre-existing inaccuracy, unrelated to the Nagios removal, found while sweeping Nagios
citations out of the same file. Left as found deliberately: correcting a group header is
a different edit from a citation sweep, and mixing them makes the sweep's diff harder to
audit for functional changes.

Low priority -- it misleads a reader of the file, nothing more. But this header is exactly
what someone consults before adding a new textfile collector, so a wrong tier count leads
to a collector landing in the wrong cadence group.

* DONE [#C] session-gather needs id_rsync installed on hera, clio, vps and andoria
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-ep6
:LABELS:   []

:FINISHED: [2026-08-15 Sat 04:44]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
session-gather.service and .timer are deployed (commit 9878b3483) and the timer
is armed for 02:30 nightly, but the job CANNOT SUCCEED until the id_rsync public
key is installed on each source host. Until then the nightly run fails, raising a
failed unit and SystemdServiceFailed.

Key created on vulcan 2026-08-14: /home/johnw/.ssh/id_rsync, ed25519, mode 600,
no passphrase (required for unattended use).
  fingerprint SHA256:oO6GcyEo+c7vRfDPuUAaX1N7ycuhS+pzh9YK5cFbYlM

Measured reachability from vulcan as johnw BEFORE the key existed:
  hera        connects and authenticates, but its authorized_keys entry for
              id_vulcan is
              from="192.168.1.2",restrict,port-forwarding,
              permitopen="andoria-08:22",command="/usr/bin/false"
              -- jump-only. That is why `ssh hera true` exits 1 with no output
              and why rsync from hera dies with exit 12 / 0 bytes.
  clio        directly reachable; only Permission denied (publickey).
  vps         directly reachable; host key accepted via TOFU; only
              Permission denied (publickey).
  andoria     not routable from vulcan and absent from johnw's ssh config;
              reached only by forwarding through hera, as pushme does.

WHAT JOHN NEEDS TO ADD (each entry confines the key to read-only rsync under one
subtree via rrsync, which is pkgs.rrsync -- NOT part of pkgs.rsync):

  ~/src/nix   config/darwin.nix, shared keys list, covers hera AND clio:
              from="192.168.1.2",restrict,port-forwarding,
              permitopen="andoria-t2:22",
              command="${pkgs.rrsync}/bin/rrsync -ro /Users/johnw"
              One entry serves both roles on hera: the forced command governs
              exec channels while forwarding is separate, exactly as the
              existing jump key works.

  ~/src/vps   modules/users/johnw.nix (needs pkgs in scope):
              restrict,command="${pkgs.rrsync}/bin/rrsync -ro /home/johnw"
              NO from= restriction -- vulcan reaches the VPS over the public
              internet through NAT, so its apparent source is a dynamic WAN
              address. Verified: vulcan has no tunnel interfaces at all, and the
              route to the OVH host leaves via end0's default gateway. A
              from="192.168.1.2" there would reject every connection.

  andoria     /home/jwiegley/.ssh/authorized_keys on the NFS-shared home. The
              ~/src/andoria repo is a home-manager config that manages no ssh
              keys, so today's pushme entry was placed by hand. It can be made
              declarative with home.file.".ssh/authorized_keys" following the
              repo's existing materializeHostSshConfig idiom -- but that
              replaces the whole file, so the existing pushme entry must be
              folded in.
              from="192.168.1.2",restrict,
              command="${pkgs.rrsync}/bin/rrsync -ro /home/jwiegley"
              /home/jwiegley covers both jobs, so this key can also replace
              id_positron for pushme.

TRAP: hera's current permitopen names andoria-08:22, but sources.vulcan.tsv uses
andoria-t2 as transport. The forward would be refused. Either widen permitopen to
andoria-t2:22 or switch the transport column back to andoria-08.

OPEN QUESTIONS FOR JOHN:
  - rrsync -ro /Users/johnw grants read of the WHOLE home, not just session
    dirs, because the roots are scattered dotdirs and rrsync takes one root. A
    wrapper validating SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND against an allowlist would be
    tighter.
  - from="192.168.1.2" is vulcan's WIRED address; it also has 192.168.3.16 on
    wifi. If traffic ever egresses via wifi these keys silently stop matching.
  - clio is pinned to 192.168.1.5 so gathering only happens on the LAN, as
    requested. Consequence: the gatherer returns non-zero if any row fails, so
    the unit FAILS every night clio is away. Decide whether clio's absence
    should be success (skip) or a real failure.
  - should pushme switch from its SOPS-stored key to id_rsync?
#+end_example

** Notes

#+begin_example
2026-08-14 16:45 -- STATUS: 1 of 4 source hosts working.

  hera        OK, 46 entries listed over rsync with id_rsync.
  clio        rc=255 "No route to host" at the pinned 192.168.1.5. Verified
              clio is unreachable at BOTH 192.168.1.5 and 10.6.0.2, so it is
              simply down rather than on the VPN. The pin is doing exactly what
              was asked -- no gathering off-LAN -- but this is also a live
              demonstration of the open question below: tonight's run would fail
              on clio alone even with every other host fixed.
  vps         rc=255 Permission denied (publickey). id_rsync not installed yet.
  andoria-t2  rc=255 "channel 0: open failed: administratively prohibited".
              hera's permitopen still names andoria-08:22 while the transport is
              andoria-t2.

The gatherer-side blockers found earlier are FIXED and pushed to GitHub as
21cd324 (sessions repo): --protect-args removed because rrsync disables it
outright, and the 24 remote rows rewritten to home-relative paths because rrsync
resolves requests beneath its own root. All 17 rows on the reachable hosts
resolved with 0 failures at the time of that fix, including the space-bearing
"Library/Application Support/..." paths. So the remaining work is entirely
key/permitopen installation, not code.

STILL NEEDS JOHN:
  1. install id_rsync on vps (no from= restriction -- vulcan reaches it over the
     public internet through NAT, so its apparent source is a dynamic WAN
     address, not 192.168.1.2);
  2. widen hera's permitopen to andoria-t2:22, or switch the transport column
     back to andoria-08;
  3. decide whether clio being away should count as failure. As built the
     gatherer returns non-zero if any row fails, so the unit fails every night
     clio is off-LAN -- which, as of this cycle, it is.

2026-08-14 20:05 -- CONFIG-ORDERING BUG FOUND AND FIXED (would have broken
andoria even after the key landed).

The job's ssh_config had `Host *` FIRST. OpenSSH keeps the first value it
obtains for each keyword, so the wildcard's `User johnw` was taken before the
`Host andoria-08` block was read, and andoria's `User jwiegley` was silently
discarded. Proven with ssh -G rather than by reading the file:

    before   andoria-08  user=johnw
    after    andoria-08  user=jwiegley  hostname=andoria-08  proxyjump=hera

Every andoria transfer would have failed with an authentication error even once
id_rsync was installed there, and it would have looked like a key problem rather
than an ordering one. Host blocks now precede the wildcard; hera/clio/vps still
resolve to johnw and all four still take IdentityFile id_rsync. Fixed in nixos
92ce570cb.

Remaining blockers unchanged, both on John's side:
  - hera's id_rsync entry needs port-forwarding + permitopen="andoria-08:22"
    (currently grants rrsync only; id_vulcan forwards, id_rsync does not);
  - vps needs id_rsync installed, with no from= restriction.
A Markdown prompt covering both, plus andoria's authorized_keys, was handed to
John on 2026-08-14 for execution from hera.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
CORRECTION to the close reason above. One line was mangled when it was written:
the phrase naming the ssh_config ordering bug contained backticks, which bash
expanded as command substitution before obr ever saw the text, deleting the very
token the sentence was about. It should read:

  - vulcan's ssh_config put the "Host *" wildcard block FIRST, so its
    "User johnw" overrode andoria's "User jwiegley" -- OpenSSH keeps the FIRST
    value it obtains for each keyword, not the most specific one. Fixed in nixos
    92ce570cb; ssh -G then resolved andoria-08 to user=jwiegley.

Recorded rather than silently left wrong, because that entry is the review trail
for a bug that would otherwise look like a key problem.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

#+begin_example

    then collapsed back onto id_rsync alone once John granted port-forwarding

because of --checksum, so the ~50-62 min / ~125 GB cost does not decay.

:LABELS:   []

:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-19 Wed 01:03]
:CLOSE_REASON: Transient remote condition, not a config defect. The configured rsync path was verified working by probing with rsync only, and a real run completed in 7m16s with exit 0.
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
Alert: SystemdServiceFailed for pushme-positron.service, 2026-08-19 00:04.
Diagnosed, NOT fixed -- the fault is on the remote machine, and this host cannot
deploy a config change right now anyway (nixos-chl).

ROOT CAUSE, exactly. The remote-side rsync binary named by --rsync-path is gone:

    bash: line 1: <home>/.nix-profile/bin/rsync: No such file or directory
    rsync: connection unexpectedly closed (0 bytes received so far) [Receiver]
    rsync error: error in rsync protocol data stream (code 12)

The unit ran for ONE SECOND (00:02:28 -> 00:02:29, exit 1). That matters because
this service is on the ServiceStuckActivating exclusion list for being a
legitimately long job with a 12h TimeoutStartSec -- so the obvious hypothesis
(a wedged or timed-out transfer) is wrong. It failed instantly on a missing
remote binary.

NOT A LOCAL REGRESSION. modules/services/pushme-positron.nix has not changed
since 2026-08-13, and the --rsync-path line itself since 2026-08-11. Over the
retained journal there are 123 successful runs against 18 "done (with errors)",
and this is the FIRST "No such file or directory" in 7 days.

WINDOW, bounded tightly by the hourly timer:
    Aug 18 23:00:59 -> 23:05:26   SUCCESS (4m27s, normal)
    Aug 19 00:02:28 -> 00:02:29   FAILED  (1s)

So the remote's ~/.nix-profile/bin/rsync disappeared between 23:05 and 00:02.
A nix garbage collection, a profile rebuild, or a home-manager switch on that
machine in that window all fit. Determining which requires looking at the remote,
which is deliberately out of scope here: John's standing rule is that the only
command this process should ever run on that host is `rsync`.

WHY THE PATH IS HARDCODED, from the module's own comment: the remote's rsync is
a Nix profile binary and is NOT on the PATH of the non-login shell that
rsync-over-ssh gets. `command -v rsync` was measured there as
<home>/.nix-profile/bin/rsync. Without the flag the transfer dies with
"rsync: command not found". So the hardcoding was deliberate and correct at the
time -- it is simply brittle against any change to the remote's profile layout.

OPTIONS:

  1. Restore rsync on the remote (reinstall into that profile). No change here
     at all, and the hourly job resumes by itself. Correct if the disappearance
     was accidental.

  2. Make --rsync-path resilient instead of exact, e.g.

       --rsync-path=PATH=$HOME/.nix-profile/bin:/etc/profiles/per-user/$USER/bin:/run/current-system/sw/bin:$PATH rsync

     This survives all three common Nix layouts, including the home-manager
     `useUserPackages = true` case where binaries move from ~/.nix-profile/bin
     to /etc/profiles/per-user/<user>/bin -- which would produce EXACTLY this
     symptom. Still a single rsync invocation, so it respects the standing
     constraint. UNTESTED: it cannot be verified while nixos-chl blocks
     switching, and it must not be committed unverified.

  3. Both -- fix the remote now, harden the flag when the host can build again.

IMPACT WHILE OPEN: the hourly pull into /tank/Backups/Contracts/<remote>/nfs is
not running, so that mirror is frozen at the Aug 18 23:05 state. The local ZFS
snapshots of it remain intact; nothing has been deleted. The alert is ACTIVE in
Alertmanager and, because the timer retries hourly and fails in a second each
time, it will keep re-notifying until either the remote is fixed or the alert is
silenced.

NOTE: no dedicated staleness alert covers this mirror -- SystemdServiceFailed is
the only thing watching it. That is adequate while the unit fails loudly, but it
would NOT catch the case where the unit succeeds while transferring nothing.
#+end_example

* DEFER [#E] Verify journal retention actually reaches 30 days after the SystemMaxUse 2G->12G fix
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-f9i
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: task
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 08:47]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-20 Thu 12:57]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:DEFERRED: [2026-09-19 Sat 09:00]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

Follow-up to the SystemMaxUse fix applied 2026-08-20. The change is verified; the
OUTCOME cannot be verified for 30 days, and this issue exists so that gap does not
quietly become an assumption.

WHAT WAS WRONG: modules/core/crash-debug.nix declared MaxRetentionSec=30day and
MaxFileSec=1month, but also SystemMaxUse=2G. journald vacuums on size before time,
so the 2G cap governed and the time settings were dead letters. Measured before
changing: journal at 1.9G against the 2G cap, and the oldest entry for every busy
system unit was 2026-08-14 15:05 -- sshd, postgresql and fetchmail-good all began
exactly there despite running for months. Effective retention ~6 days against 30
declared, an ~80% shortfall.

FIXED: SystemMaxUse=2G -> 12G, sized from the observed ~317 MB/day (1.9G over 6
days, a window that INCLUDES the 08-14..08-17 matter-server storm, so a busy-period
rate rather than a quiet one). 30 days at that rate is ~9.5G.

VERIFIED NOW, from journald's own accounting rather than the config file:
  "System Journal (...) is 1.9G, max 12G, 10G free."
journald restarted cleanly (Result=success) and no longer reports the cap as
binding.

NOT VERIFIED, and cannot be until ~2026-09-19: that retention actually reaches 30
days. Raising the cap does not restore already-vacuumed data -- everything before
2026-08-14 is permanently gone -- so the window grows forward from today only.

CHECK ON OR AFTER 2026-09-19:
  sudo journalctl -u sshd.service -o short-iso --no-pager -q | head -1
  sudo journalctl --disk-usage
Expect the oldest sshd entry to be ~30 days old and usage below 12G. If usage is
pinned at 12G with retention under 30 days, the daily rate is higher than measured
and the cap needs raising again -- or the noisy unit needs finding first.

WHY THIS MATTERED MORE THAN A TIDINESS FIX: this host's retrospective practice
leans on the journal -- "when did this unit start failing", "did this alert fire
last week". At 6 days those questions return truncated answers that look exactly
like clean results. It bit me directly in this cycle: I read fetchmail-good's
failures as beginning on 08-15 with "nothing in the 29 days before", which was
retention, not onset. The unit has existed since 2025-11-06.

DISCOVERED-FROM: chasing two fetchmail-good socket errors in the 2026-08-20 health
cycle, which turned out to be benign and self-recovering.

* DONE [#E] Hung Zimit crawl is undetected: ServiceStuckActivating excluded, and no progress-based detector exists
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-fdi
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: task
:CREATED:  [2026-08-20 Thu 16:51]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-20 Thu 17:51]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-20 Thu 17:51]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

A Zimit crawl that HANGS WHILE RUNNING is now undetected. This is a gap I opened
deliberately on 2026-08-20 and is recorded here rather than left implicit.

WHAT CHANGED: zimit-job-runner was excluded from ServiceStuckActivating
(alerts/systemd.yaml). It is a Type=oneshot that runs an entire crawl synchronously,
so it sits in `activating` for the whole job and tripped the 15m threshold on every
normal run. Measured across 22 completed jobs, legitimate durations span 26 MINUTES
to 43 DAYS (ocean-of-lights, 1039.32h), and TimeoutStartSec is already 4w2d, so
month-long crawls are tolerated by design. An alert firing for the entire duration of
every crawl is noise, and noise is what trains people to ignore a rule.

WHY NOTHING ELSE COVERS IT -- checked, not assumed:
  ZimitJobsFailed  fires on the failed-jobs counter; a hung job has not failed.
  ZimitJobsStuck   fires on zimit_jobs_pending > 0 for 24h. That is a QUEUE that
                   will not drain. A hung RUNNING job has pending=0 and running=1,
                   so this rule cannot see it.
  TimeoutStartSec  4w2d. It would eventually fail the unit, but 30 days is not
                   detection.

WHY I DID NOT JUST PICK A DURATION THRESHOLD: the legitimate range admits none.
26 minutes to 43 days is a ~2400x spread, and the longest legitimate crawl exceeds
the unit timeout. Any threshold either fires on real crawls or never fires at all.
Inventing one would have produced exactly the kind of unjustifiable rule this repo
keeps having to remediate.

THE RIGHT SIGNAL IS PROGRESS, NOT DURATION. The runner logs continuously while
crawling -- measured live during the llm-primer job at ~280 lines per rolling 2
minutes, and browsertrix emits structured JSON with context "crawlStatus" /
"pageStatus" on every page. A crawl that stops advancing stops logging, while a
healthy multi-day crawl keeps logging throughout. So the detector wants to be either:
  (a) a textfile collector exporting last-progress-timestamp for the running job,
      alerting when now - last_progress > ~30m while zimit_jobs_running > 0; or
  (b) a Loki rule over {unit="zimit-job-runner.service"} counting crawl-progress
      lines in a window and alerting on zero while a job is running. This host
      already runs Loki rules for exactly this shape (matter-fabric.yaml).
(b) is cheaper -- no new exporter, and the logs already reach Loki -- but note the
matter-fabric precedent's warnings: Loki-ruler alerts never appear in Prometheus's
ALERTS series, so verify with loki_prometheus_notifications_sent_total instead.

NOT URGENT: a hung crawl wastes time and a container, it does not lose data or
degrade the host. Filed so the exclusion is not silently mistaken for coverage --
the comment in alerts/systemd.yaml says the same and points here.

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
Implemented a progress-based detector, which is what this issue said was needed.

WHAT WAS BUILT:
,  * zimit_running_job_log_age_seconds{job_id,name} — new gauge on the web UI's
    /metrics endpoint. The runner tees crawl output to
    $WORK_DIR/<job_id>/zimit.log, so that file's mtime advances while pages are
    being fetched and stops dead when a crawl wedges.
,  * ZimitCrawlStalled — alerts/zimit.yaml, expr
    zimit_running_job_log_age_seconds > 1800, for: 5m.

EMITTED ONLY FOR RUNNING JOBS, deliberately. The series vanishes when nothing is
crawling, so the rule cannot fire against an idle host and needs no join against
zimit_jobs_running.

THRESHOLD SIZED BY REPLAY over three real crawl logs, and THE FIRST SAMPLE ALONE
WOULD HAVE BEEN BADLY WRONG:
    a03d993b (live,  14,483 lines)   median 0.17s   max   7.8s
    9c70095e (     109,127 lines)    median 0.11s   max  89.9s
    1d03bff6 (     549,120 lines)    median 0.02s   max 180.0s
Sampling only the live crawl suggested healthy gaps never exceed ~8s. Two more
logs showed real gaps up to 180s -- and the 89.9s/180.0s maxima recur as EXACT
repeated values across hundreds of thousands of lines, which is the fingerprint of
browsertrix page timeouts rather than random stalls. So a healthy crawl genuinely
goes three minutes without writing. 1800s is 10x the worst observed gap across
~673,000 lines: wide enough for a longer timeout on some future site, tight enough
to catch a hang within the half hour instead of never.

WHY NOT DURATION, restated because it is the trap: legitimate crawls run 26
MINUTES to 43 DAYS and TimeoutStartSec is 4w2d, so no duration threshold separates
healthy from hung. That is why ServiceStuckActivating had to be excluded rather
than retuned.

VERIFIED against the live llm-primer crawl:
,  * endpoint emits zimit_running_job_log_age_seconds{job_id="a03d993b",
    name="llm-primer"} 0
,  * Prometheus scrapes it: 1 series
,  * rule reads health=ok, lastError empty, state=inactive
,  * NOT a dead rule -- positive-controlled: with an always-true threshold (> -1)
    the expression returns 1 series, and at the real threshold (> 1800) it
    correctly returns 0 while the crawl is progressing.
,  * zimit-web-ui stayed active across the change.

NOT VERIFIED, and it cannot be without wedging a real crawl: that the alert fires
on an actual hang. What is verified is that the metric tracks real progress, the
comparison mechanics work, and the rule cannot fire spuriously.
#+end_example

:END:

#+begin_example
Install Grist (gristlabs/grist-core) as a rootless quadlet container, integrated
with the host's PostgreSQL and Redis, behind nginx+TLS at grist.vulcan.lan.

All configuration is written and evaluates on BOTH paths (verified with
nix eval + extendModules):
  - disabled: grist absent from home-manager.users, so the container-health
    exporter and the container image updater correctly ignore it
  - enabled: nginx vhost, redis 6388, both SOPS secrets with correct
    owner/mode/path, firewall on podman0, tmpfiles (d/Z, never D),
    postgresql-grist-setup unit, Glance tile, blackbox target

Files: modules/containers/grist-quadlet.nix (new, system side + enable switch),
modules/users/home-manager/grist.nix (new, quadlet), plus edits to
databases.nix, quadlet.nix, glance.nix, blackbox-monitoring.nix,
certs/renew-nginx-certs.sh, docs/ports.txt, hosts/vulcan/default.nix.

Deliberately NOT built: no Grafana dashboard and no Nagios check. Grist exposes
no Prometheus endpoint (gristlabs/grist-core#671 is an open feature request), and
Nagios is being removed from this host. Coverage is container_health_status +
blackbox HTTPS + cert expiry. Storage is a plain /var/lib directory by John's
choice, so no Samba share and no ZFS snapshots.

BLOCKED on two things only John can do:
  1. Two SOPS keys in secrets.yaml: grist-db-password and grist-secrets
     (env file with GRIST_SESSION_SECRET and TYPEORM_PASSWORD; TYPEORM_PASSWORD
     must equal grist-db-password).
  2. Issuing the TLS certificate for grist.vulcan.lan. Nginx refuses to start
     when a vhost references a certificate that does not exist, so this must
     happen before the switch that flips services.grist.enable = true.

Then: enable, /etc/nixos/build switch, and test end to end.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
Deployed and working. John added both SOPS keys and issued the certificate on
2026-08-19; enabling it surfaced two real defects, both now fixed.

DEFECT 1: the PostgreSQL ROLE was never declared. postgresql-grist-setup only SETS
a password; it failed outright with `role "grist" does not exist`. mkPostgresUserSetup
had been copied from NocoBase without its counterpart entries in
services.postgresql.ensureDatabases / ensureUsers. Both now added, gated the same way.

DEFECT 2, the more interesting one: the container wrote to /var/lib/grist as
subuid 2394760, not as grist(899). Rootless podman maps container uid 0 to the
invoking host user, so an image running as root lands files as that user with no
configuration -- which is why /var/lib/nocobase is cleanly owned by nocobase(945)
and this never came up before. Grist's image drops to uid 1000, and 1000 maps into
the SUBUID range instead (grist's base 2393760 + 1000).

That was NOT cosmetic. The `Z /var/lib/grist 0750 grist grist` rule recursively
chowns the tree back to 899 on every boot and every rebuild, and at 0750 the subuid
then has no access at all -- Grist would have silently lost write access to its own
documents at the next activation, days later, with nothing connecting cause to
effect. Confirmed by running `systemd-tmpfiles --create --prefix=/var/lib/grist` by
hand and watching it rewrite 2394760 -> 899.

Fixed by correcting the MAPPING rather than dropping the tmpfiles rule:
podmanArgs = [ "--userns=keep-id:uid=1000,gid=1000" ]. On-disk ownership now matches
what every other service here assumes, and the directory stays administrable as grist.

Also removed: home.sessionVariables.PODMAN_USERNS = "keep-id", copied from the other
container users. home.sessionVariables goes into the shell profile and is NOT
inherited by systemd user units, so it never affected any of these containers. It is
a no-op that reads like configuration.

VERIFIED END TO END:
,  * https://grist.vulcan.lan -> 302 -> 200, <title>Loading... - Grist</title>, landing
    on /o/vulcan/boot, so GRIST_SINGLE_ORG is in effect
,  * PostgreSQL: 25 tables created by Grist's own migrations in the grist database --
    which is what proves the container authenticated, not merely that a role exists
,  * Redis 6388: 8 keys, so the session store is live rather than falling back to a
    container-local SQLite file
,  * blackbox probe probe_success=1; container_running=1, container_restart_count=0,
    container_health_status=3 (= "none", no HEALTHCHECK in the image; 15 of 17
    containers here read 3, and ContainerUnhealthy fires on == 1, so this does not
    false-alarm)
,  * Glance tile present in the live /run/glance/glance.yaml next to NocoBase
,  * write path proven, not assumed: touching a file inside the container shows
    1000:1000 in-container and 899:891 on the host, and it survives a tmpfiles run
    unchanged
,  * 0 failed units afterwards
#+end_example

    - john@bia.bahai.org   (google): setup_error

The cause is in the Home Assistant log, dated 2026-08-17 12:46:
    Error communicating with API: Access token failure: 400,
    message='Bad Request', url='https://www.googleapis.com/oauth2/v4/token'
i.e. the OAuth refresh token is being rejected. That is the signature of a
refresh token that has expired or been revoked, not of a network fault.

STRONGLY SUSPECTED CAUSE, worth checking before re-authenticating: a Google
OAuth consent screen left in "Testing" publishing status expires its refresh
tokens every 7 DAYS. This host has been bitten by exactly that before -- it is
why four rclone cloud-drive remotes were disabled (project_cloud_drive_backup)
and it is the same weekly-expiry shape as the Schwab token for stock-trader. If
the Google Cloud project behind these entries is still in Testing, re-auth will
buy another 7 days and no more; publishing the app (or making it Internal to a
Workspace org) is the durable fix.

THE MONITORING GAP, which is the part worth fixing here. Nothing in Prometheus
watches this:
,  * hass-integration-exporter tracks 12 integrations -- august, cast,
    enphase_envoy, flume, homekit, lg_thinq, miele, nest, nws, ring, webostv,
    withings -- and `google` is not among them.
,  * ADDING google TO THAT LIST WOULD NOT HELP, and I nearly did it before
    reading the metric's own HELP text: hass_integration_loaded is "1 if the
    integration appears in Home Assistant's LOADED COMPONENTS, 0 if not". The
    google COMPONENT is loaded; it is the three config ENTRIES that failed. The
    metric would report 1 and detect nothing -- a no-op that looks like
    coverage.
,  * So the exporter measures the wrong thing for this failure mode. Detecting it
    needs config-entry state (what Node-RED's flow already reads), not component
    load. That is true for every integration in the list, not just google: any
    of the 12 could have a failed entry while reporting loaded=1.

The only thing that noticed was a Node-RED flow writing to the journal, where it
sat unread for two days.

NOT FIXED, and split by who can do it:
,  * Re-authenticating the three accounts requires John's Google logins.
,  * Extending monitoring to config-entry state is real work (a new exporter or a
    reshaped one) and changes what the 12 existing series mean, so it wants a
    decision rather than a quiet edit.

Related: the same day (Aug 16) also produced two `Error setting climate.upstairs
temperature` failures with a heat_cool RANGE payload, and the Nest's own
google_nest_sdm AuthException. Those have not recurred since, so they look like
one bad day for Google auth on this host rather than an ongoing fault.
#+end_example

** Notes

OBSERVATION 2026-08-19 23:50, not a close: the setup_error entries are GONE.

hass_config_entries has been emitting since 18:48 today, and over its whole life
there have been ZERO entries in setup_error, migration_error or failed_unload. The
only non-loaded states are the seven legitimately disabled entries (bluetooth,
bmw_connected_drive, bose, kumo, llama_conversation, radio_browser, one google)
plus four setup_in_progress samples at 19:28 that are HA restarting after my build
-- transient by definition and correctly not alerted on.

So the three Google Calendar entries recovered before the metric existed,
consistent with John having re-authenticated. HomeAssistantConfigEntryFailed has
never fired: no ALERTS samples in 24h, which for this rule means it has had nothing
to fire about, not that it is broken (it read health=ok with empty lastError when
deployed).

LEAVING OPEN DELIBERATELY. The symptom is gone; the question the issue exists for
is not. If the Google Cloud OAuth consent screen is still in Testing publishing
status, re-auth buys exactly 7 days and these entries return around 2026-08-24.
That date is the falsification test: silence through it means the app is published
or Internal and this is genuinely closed; a recurrence means the durable fix is the
publishing-status change, not another re-auth.

The monitoring half of this issue is done and now covers the recurrence
automatically -- which is the whole reason a recurrence would be visible this time
rather than sitting unnoticed for two days.

:PROPERTIES:

:LABELS:   []

:FINISHED: [2026-08-12 Wed 11:26]

:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

'Ruhi Book 10' exists on both calendar.baha_i and calendar.jwiegley_gmail_com.
Each has its own ha-events-calendar pair on the Office tab: a 'Before' node (offset -3 minutes) and a
'Start' node (offset +1 minute). So the blue WiZ notification fires twice at T-3min and twice at T+1min.

Harmless today because both set the same colour and the action is idempotent, but it would matter for
anything non-idempotent. Consider deduplicating (a join/gate on event summary) if it becomes a problem.

** Close Reason

Won't fix, by John's decision 2026-08-12. 'Ruhi Book 10' exists on both calendar.baha_i and calendar.jwiegley_gmail_com, and each has its own ha-events-calendar pair on the Office tab (Before at -3min, Start at +1min), so a single event fires the blue WiZ notification twice at T-3min and twice at T+1min.

Left as-is because the action is IDEMPOTENT: both paths call light.turn_on with the same rgb_color on the same two bulbs, so the visible result is identical whether it fires once or twice. A dedupe gate would add flow state to a chain that currently has none, for no observable benefit — and flow context resets on every deploy.

IF THIS CHANGES: attaching anything non-idempotent to that chain (a TTS announcement, a counter, a toggle, a notification to a phone) will double-fire. At that point the fix is a gate keyed on event summary + start time, or a filter on one of the two ha-events-calendar nodes (they have an unused 'filter' field). The duplication is in the calendar data, not the flows — each node is correctly watching its own calendar.

* DONE [#D] systemd-tmpfiles exits 73 every run: unsafe path transition on the Immich media directory
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-g52
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-17 Mon 12:48]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-19 Wed 18:13]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-19 Wed 18:13]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
systemd-tmpfiles-setup.service and systemd-tmpfiles-resetup.service both exit 73
(EX_CANTCREAT) on every run, while reporting Result=success. Found during the
2026-08-17 health cycle by scanning for ExecMainStatus != 0, because
`systemctl --failed` cannot see this class at all.

THE FAILING RULE. Generated by the upstream nixpkgs immich module from
services.immich.mediaLocation (modules/services/immich.nix:46), landing in
/etc/tmpfiles.d/immich.conf as:

    'e' '/tank/Photos/Immich' '0700' 'immich' 'immich' '-'

systemd refuses it:

    Detected unsafe path transition /tank/Photos (owned by johnw) ->
    /tank/Photos/Immich (owned by immich) during canonicalization of
    tank/Photos/Immich.

That is a deliberate systemd hardening: it will not traverse into a directory
whose owner differs from its parent's unless the parent is root-owned. Actual
state is /tank/Photos = johnw:immich 2750, /tank/Photos/Immich = immich:immich
750. Both are separate ZFS datasets.

WHY THIS IS FILED RATHER THAN FIXED -- the failure may be load-bearing.

The rule wants mode 0700. It currently never applies, so the directory sits at
750. /tank/Photos/Immich is ALSO exported as an SMB share (modules/services/
samba.nix:101-102, where each entry becomes a share via `path = fs.mountpoint`
at line 155). Making the rule succeed would tighten the directory to 0700
immich:immich, which is exactly the kind of change that breaks share access
quietly and is noticed days later.

Mitigating: `getent group immich` lists no supplementary members, so johnw
cannot traverse a 750 immich:immich directory today either. The SMB share is
therefore probably already inaccessible to him, meaning the practical blast
radius of "fixing" it may be nil. PROBABLY is doing real work in that sentence
and is precisely why this needs John rather than a guess.

CURRENT IMPACT: no functional breakage found. immich-server and
immich-machine-learning are both active. The cost is that two system units
carry a permanent nonzero exit, so a NEW tmpfiles rule failing later would be
indistinguishable from this one -- the journal for the current boot shows this
transition as the only failure, so nothing is masked yet.

THE OPTIONS:

  1. chown /tank/Photos to root:immich (keeping 2750). A root-owned parent
     makes the transition safe by systemd's rule and the rule applies. Changes
     ownership of a directory holding John's photos, so it is his call.

  2. Drop the upstream rule via systemd.tmpfiles.settings, overriding immich's
     generated entry. Silences the exit without touching data, and records that
     0700 is deliberately not wanted here.

  3. Accept it and suppress nothing. Cheapest, but leaves the masking hazard.

DO NOT act without John: options 1 and 2 both change permissions or ownership
semantics on a data directory that is also a network share.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
Fixed and verified live. The first attempt (2026-08-18, chown /tank/Photos to root)
was wrong twice over and both errors are now corrected.

WHAT WAS WRONG WITH IT. systemd's rule (chase.c, unsafe_transition) is: a step is
safe iff the parent is root-owned OR parent and child share an owner. It applies to
EVERY edge, and to the final component too -- not just intermediate ones, which is
what I had assumed. So making /tank/Photos root-owned only moved the failure up:
"unsafe path transition /tank (owned by johnw) -> /tank/Photos (owned by root)".
With /tank owned by johnw and the media directory owned by immich, no ownership for
/tank/Photos satisfies both edges. The change also locked its own rule out: `z
/tank/Photos` could no longer run once /tank/Photos was root-owned, so it was
unappliable from then on. And it cost johnw the write bit on his own photo
directory (verified: `test -w /tank/Photos` failed as johnw).

THE FIX.
  1. Dropped upstream's tmpfiles entry entirely --
     systemd.tmpfiles.settings.immich."/tank/Photos/Immich" = lib.mkForce { }.
     No rule below /tank/Photos means no canonicalization through it.
  2. Replaced its guarantee with a root-privileged ExecStartPre on immich-server
     (chown immich:immich + chmod 0750), which does not canonicalize, so the
     drift protection is kept rather than quietly dropped.
  3. Reverted /tank/Photos to johnw:immich 2750, both in the rule and on disk
     (the on-disk chown was needed by hand because the rule had locked itself out).

NOT DONE, and left for John: chown root /tank would make every rule in the chain
work and is the other real fix, but /tank is the pool mountpoint, its ownership is
on-disk state no module declares, and taking it from johnw changes who can create
at the top of the pool. That is his call, not a side effect of an Immich fix.

VERIFIED after /etc/nixos/build switch:
,  * systemd-tmpfiles-resetup: ExecMainStatus=0, Result=success, no unsafe/fail
    lines in its journal for that run (was 73 on every run for months)
,  * `systemd-tmpfiles --dry-run --create --boot` exits 0 with no failures, which
    is what proves the next boot is clean without rebooting to find out
,  * systemd-tmpfiles-setup still reads 73, and that is stale, not unfixed: it is a
    boot-only unit whose InactiveExitTimestamp is 2026-07-03 11:37:53, the current
    boot. It clears at the next boot.
,  * /etc/tmpfiles.d/immich.conf renders empty; no tmpfiles rule mentions
    /tank/Photos/Immich anywhere
,  * /tank/Photos johnw:immich 2750, /tank/Photos/Immich immich:immich 0750
,  * johnw can write /tank/Photos again and traverse /tank/Photos/Immich
,  * immich-server active, ExecMainStatus=0, both ExecStartPre lines present in the
    deployed unit
#+end_example

    "issue_id": "nixos-g52",
    "author": "johnw",
    "text": "PARTIALLY FIXED, and I got the analysis wrong. Reopening.\n\nDeployed in the 2026-08-19 11:54 switch. What worked:\n    /tank/Photos          -> root:immich 2750          as intended\n    /tank/Photos/Immich   -> immich:immich 750         mode override applied\n    johnw groups          -> johnw wheel immich        group grant landed\n    johnw traverses both  -> YES                       the SMB share is usable\n    immich-server / -ml   -> both active\nThe deployed tmpfiles rule confirms the override reached it:\n    'e' '/tank/Photos/Immich' '0750' 'immich' 'immich' '-'\n\nWHAT DID NOT WORK: systemd-tmpfiles-setup and -resetup STILL exit 73. I moved\nthe fault rather than removing it:\n\n    before   Detected unsafe path transition /tank/Photos (owned by johnw)\n             -> /tank/Photos/Immich (owned by immich)\n    now      Detected unsafe path transition /tank (owned by johnw)\n             -> /tank/Photos (owned by root)\n\nMY ERROR: systemd refuses to traverse into a directory whose owner differs from\nits parent's unless the parent is root-owned. I applied that rule at the\nchild boundary and never checked the one above it. Making /tank/Photos\nroot-owned satisfied the /tank/Photos -> Immich edge and simultaneously created\na NEW violation on the /tank -> /tank/Photos edge, because /tank is johnw-owned.\nThe chain has to be safe at EVERY edge, not just the one in the error message.\n\nSo the user-visible goals are met -- johnw reaches the Immich directory, the SMB\nshare works, permissions are as decided -- but the original symptom, two units\nexiting 73 on every run, is unchanged.\n\nWHAT REMAINS TO DECIDE. Following the same logic upward means chowning /tank\nitself to root, which is a much larger change to the pool root and not something\nto do on the back of this. The alternatives are to cancel upstream's rule\nentirely (the option originally offered as \"override the rule away\", which\ntouches no ownership and would remove the exit 73 outright), or to accept the\nexit code and document it. Both are John's call; the first is what I should have\nrecommended more strongly given it never needed the chown at all.\n",
    "created_at": "2026-08-19T19:35:20Z"
  }
]
#+end_src

* DONE [#B] Syncthing Documents folder stuck 6 days: chmod clobbered the ACL mask
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-g9y
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-14 Fri 17:56]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-14 Fri 17:56]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-14 Fri 17:56]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

/tank/Documents stopped syncing from hera on 2026-08-08 19:37 and sat broken for
six days. John noticed documents present on hera and clio that never appeared on
vulcan.

    state         error
    error         stat /tank/Documents/.stfolder: permission denied
    needFiles     642        needBytes 1738449009 (1.74 GB)
    pullErrors    453

CAUSE: chmod rewrites a directory's POSIX ACL mask. syncthing-folder-acls runs

    chmod 0700 /tank/Documents
    setfacl -m u:syncthing:rwX /tank/Documents

and the chmod leaves mask::--- , which reduces every named entry to
"#effective:---". getfacl still SHOWS user:syncthing:rwx, so the grant looks
correct and does nothing. Any later chmod re-breaks it.

The damage spread through the tree: 131 of 400 sampled entries carried the
clobbered mask, and the pull errors were dominated by
"mkdir /tank/Documents/positron-sow/.obr: permission denied" and similar.

Not a clio problem: clio is deliberately not a device on this folder. Clio syncs
to hera, and hera syncs to vulcan, so clio's documents arrive by that path -- and
were blocked by the same fault.

** Close Reason

Fixed at both levels and verified against the live folder.

IMMEDIATE: restored the mask on /tank/Documents, then on .stignore (a second
blocker that only surfaced once the directory was readable), then recursively
across the tree once pull errors showed the damage was tree-wide.

Recovery, measured at each step:
    broken      state=error   needFiles=642  pullErrors=0    (wedged, not trying)
    after dir   state=error   needFiles=642  error moved to .stignore
    after file  state=syncing needFiles=319  pullErrors=14   localFiles 27257->27562
    after -R    state=idle    needFiles=1    pullErrors=453->1
    after fix   state=scanning needFiles=1   pullErrors=0

DURABLE: syncthing-folder-acls now passes -m m::rwX alongside every named entry,
and -m d:m::rwx on directories. Verified by RE-RUNNING the service, which
performs the very chmod that used to break it: mask::rwx survived and
user:syncthing:rwx is effective rather than '#effective:---'. Before the change
that same re-run is what produced the failure.

Setting the mask grants nothing new -- it only re-enables the u: entries named
on the same line. Confirmed after every step that group:: and other:: remain
--- , and that nobody and postgres are still denied. The unix mode displays 770
rather than 700 because the ACL mask occupies the group bits; the real group
entry is group::--- .

One incidental note: I called /rest/db/override during recovery, which is a
send-only operation and should not have been used on a sendreceive folder.
Verified it was a no-op -- no override line in the journal, hera's completion is
99.9999% with needDeletes=0 and remoteState=valid, so hera lost nothing.

Clio was never a member of this folder and deliberately stays out: clio syncs to
hera and hera syncs to vulcan, so clio's documents arrive by that path. They were
blocked by this same fault, not by a missing device entry.

Remaining: 1 file (2751 bytes) still converging, JumpDesktop/Viewer/Servers/...,
with 0 pull errors.

* DONE [#E] session-gather.nix cites docs/SESSION_GATHER.md, which does not exist
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-h1t
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: docs
:CREATED:  [2026-08-19 Wed 23:19]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-19 Wed 23:28]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-19 Wed 23:28]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

modules/services/session-gather.nix:24 ends its permissions comment with 'See docs/SESSION_GATHER.md.' No such file exists in the tree, and git log shows it was never committed.

This matters more than a typo because of what the comment is about: it explains that the service has read access to the configured homes and nothing else, and defers the detail to a document that isn't there. The reader who most needs the detail -- someone widening that access -- gets a dead end.

Unlike the repo's two other unresolvable docs/ citations, this one is NOT annotated at the citation site. docs/README.md:338-344 asserts that every dangling docs/ citation is annotated where it is cited; this is the counterexample.

Fix is a decision, not a strike: either write the document, or fold the durable sentence into the comment and drop the pointer. The second is what docs/README.md:93-95 recommends for exactly this shape.

DISCOVERED-FROM: re-measuring the docs/ citation survey while fixing nixos-iwh.

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
Fixed by dropping the pointer, not by writing the document.

The SECURITY comment at modules/services/session-gather.nix:21-24 already carried the entire contract it was deferring: dedicated ~/.ssh/id_rsync key, authorized_keys forced to 'rrsync -ro <home>', read-only, one subtree, no pty, no agent forwarding, and the blast radius stated outright. There was no durable sentence left in the missing document to rescue -- writing it would have duplicated the comment. docs/README.md:93-95 recommends exactly this resolution for this shape.

While there I added the one fact the comment was missing and that a reader widening this access actually needs: the forced command is enforced on the SOURCE host, so a change to this module cannot widen the confinement and a change to a source host's authorized_keys can.

TRAP HIT AND FIXED: my first version of the annotation wrote the missing path literally, which made the repo's docs-citation grep match my own prose -- recreating the phantom citation the comment exists to record. Reworded to name the document without spelling the path, and said so in the comment so the next person does not undo it.

VERIFIED:
,  * the citation survey now returns exactly ONE unresolvable docs/ path,
    docs/deploy/freshness-rollout-runbook.md, which is annotated at its citation
    site as living in the upstream stock-trader repo. The repo's invariant that no
    unresolvable docs/ citation is silent is true again.
,  * counts moved with the fix: 84 lines over 22 distinct paths, down from 85/23.
    docs/README.md was updated in the same commit, and both numbers re-measured.
,  * ./build switch produced store path zck9f8y7wdrkrjv56jl6mh8071ljffw2, byte-identical
    to the build before this edit -- comments do not survive into the derivation, so an
    identical hash is proof the change is documentation-only.
#+end_example

* TODO [#B] zone.home is set to placeholder Sacramento city-centre coords 9.3km from the house, so no GPS tracker can ever report home
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-h2a
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-09-01 Tue 12:00]
:MODIFIED: [2026-09-01 Tue 12:00]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:SOURCE_REPO: .
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

#+begin_example
THE ROOT CAUSE BEHIND THREE FALSE ALARM ARMINGS (2026-06-26, 2026-07-01, 2026-09-01).

modules/services/home-assistant.nix generates /var/lib/hass/secrets.yaml from an
activation-script heredoc carrying HARDCODED placeholder coordinates, under its own
comments: 'Location coordinates for Sacramento, CA area' and 'Update with your actual
coordinates if needed'. They were never updated. configuration.yaml resolves
zone.home via !secret latitude/longitude, so zone.home sits on the generic city
centre point -- measured distance from zone.home centre to that literal pair is
exactly 0 m -- while the house is ~9.3 km away. zone.home radius is 200 m.

CONSEQUENCE: no GPS-sourced device_tracker can ever be inside the home zone.
Measured, current, distance from zone.home (accuracy in brackets):
    johns_iphone_17_pro  9316 m  [10 m]
    johns_mac_studio     9316 m  [20 m]
    ipad_pro             9318 m  [ 7 m]
    clio_2 (car)         9301 m  [35 m]
    nasims_iphone        8060 m  [3712 m]
Across the full 29-day recorder window NO gps-source tracker has EVER reported
'home'; every entity that reports 'home' is router-sourced. johns_mac_studio is a
stationary desktop that cannot leave the house and is not_home in 27 of 27 rows.
clio_2 is a Renault reporting its own GNSS, independent of Apple's WiFi location
database, and agrees with the Apple devices to within ~15 m -- which rules out the
competing explanation that a stale WiFi-geolocation entry is displacing the devices.
The devices are right; the zone is wrong.

WHY THIS MATTERS BEYOND A COSMETIC BADGE: it silently removes all redundancy from
presence. person.john_wiegley can only ever be held 'home' by the single flap-prone
OPNsense router tracker, so one iPhone WiFi power-save drop flips whole-house
presence. That is the exact mechanism of all three false armings. It also means the
fix recommended on 2026-07-01 -- 'add the Companion GPS device_tracker to
person.john_wiegley' -- could never have worked, because the GPS tracker cannot
report home while the zone is misplaced. That recommendation should be considered
superseded by this issue.

FIX REQUIRES OPERATOR ACTION (a new SOPS secret; not created unilaterally):
  1. operator adds home-assistant/latitude and home-assistant/longitude (and
     optionally elevation) to secrets.yaml with the ACTUAL house coordinates.
     They belong in SOPS rather than the heredoc because this .nix file is tracked
     in git and a home address should not be committed in plaintext.
  2. change the activation script to render those from sops placeholders instead of
     the hardcoded pair.
  3. after rebuild, verify every gps tracker flips to 'home' with the same distance
     query, and that binary_sensor.anyone_home survives a deliberate router-tracker
     drop.

SEPARATE, COMPOUNDING, PHONE-SIDE ISSUE: sensor.john_wiegleys_iphone_location_permission
reads 'Authorized when in use', not 'Always'. ~17 location/motion-dependent Companion
sensors sit 'unavailable' and the GPS tracker updates only ~5x/day instead of
continuously. Fixing the zone alone leaves presence updating far too slowly to be
the primary source; both changes are needed for real redundancy.
#+end_example

* DEFER [#D] Outdoor plug WiFi flapping: works, but late turn-off + failed Node-RED service calls
:PROPERTIES:

switch.smart_outdoor_plug_switch_1/2 cycles off<->unavailable ~117x/day vs a 2-3/day baseline.
Onset was exactly the matter-server restart at 2026-08-10 15:18 (zero p3 errors in hours 10-14, then 183 in hour 15).
matter-server logs 'CASESession timed out ... peer <000000000000001A>' at ~1.4/min (~2000/day).

IMPORTANT correction to earlier analysis: this is a WiFi/LAN node reached over wlp1s0f0, NOT Thread.
Every genuine Thread node on this fabric advertises an fd:: mesh ULA; 0x1A advertises on the routable LAN.
So the Aqara Hub M3 border router is NOT in its path and Thread-range advice does not apply.
Check WiFi signal at the plug, AP association, and DHCP lease stability; ask what changed around Aug 10.

Do NOT restart matter-server: prior experience says it does not help, and the restart is what started this.
Alert MatterNodeSessionFailures (Loki, added 2026-08-11) now fires on this.

** Notes

#+begin_example
2026-08-20 00:5x — THE REMISSION IS OVER. MatterNodeSessionFailures is firing
again and node 26 is the sole cause.

Yesterday I recorded this plug's errors collapsing to ~0 and said explicitly that
an unexplained remission is not a fix. That held for about 26 hours.

  last-hour peer breakdown: 75 of 75 failures are peer <000000000000001A = node 26.
  Node 17 contributes zero this hour.

CASESession timeouts per hour, most recent:
  08-19 21:00  15     08-19 22:00   0
  08-19 23:00  21     08-20 00:00  54
Alert startsAt 2026-08-20T07:14:12Z (00:14 PDT).

Per calendar day, node 26 specifically:
  08-14 1577   08-15 2231   08-16 1872   08-17 782   08-18 0   08-19 21   08-20 54+

So the pattern is episodic with multi-day gaps, not a steady fault and not a
monotonic recovery. Any future claim that this is "fixed" needs to outlast a
gap of at least 2 days to mean anything, because 08-18/08-19 looked like a fix
and was not.

THE DETECTOR IS SOUND — I checked before trusting it, and my first two attempts
to check were themselves broken:
,  * Prometheus ALERTS showed no samples in 8 days, which I briefly read as "never
    fired". Wrong instrument: this is a LOKI ruler rule and its alerts never enter
    Prometheus's ALERTS series at all.
,  * The alertmanager journal showed zero mentions. Also useless -- that journal
    has zero lines total in 8 days, so it cannot answer any question.
,  * The instrument that works: loki_prometheus_notifications_sent_total shows the
    ruler sending 685-783 notifications/day across 08-13..08-17, and replaying the
    rule expression against Loki puts 107 of 124 hourly buckets over threshold at
    63-95/h. The rule has been doing its job correctly the whole time.

STILL JOHN'S CALL. Node 26 is WiFi (FeatureMap=1), so per the rule's own runbook
this points at signal strength, AP association or DHCP -- physical-world causes.
The rule also says explicitly: DO NOT reflexively restart matter-server; the
2026-08-10 flap storm began at the moment of a restart.
