* DONE [#E] mbsync transient Gmail timeouts raise critical pages (rbcca + bia, ~9/10d)
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-mwl
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: chore
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 16:39]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-15 Sat 23:16]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-15 Sat 23:16]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

mbsync-rbcca fails ~4x/week with 'Socket error on imap.gmail.com: timeout'; johnw/bia/assembly had zero
failures over the same 7 days. rbcca's maildir is 435M vs bia's 3.0M, which likely explains why only it
times out. No mail is lost (Sync Pull, idempotent, SyncState preserved) and it self-heals on the next
15-minute tick — but the unit sits 'failed' until then, raising a critical SystemdServiceFailed each time.

John chose to LEAVE IT ALONE on 2026-08-11. Recorded for the backlog only.
If revisited, note two traps: 'Restart' in extraServiceConfig fails Nix evaluation unless the base
mkMbsyncModule.nix:158 becomes lib.mkDefault (or mkForce is used), and StartLimitBurst/StartLimitIntervalSec
are [Unit]-section options that extraServiceConfig cannot carry at all.

** Notes

2026-08-15 -- NOT rbcca-only. mbsync-bia shows the same failure, so the title's
focus on one account understates the scope.

Survey of every mbsync unit over 10 days:
    mbsync-rbcca      7 failures, 7 of them "Socket error ... timeout"
    mbsync-bia        2 failures, 2 of them "Socket error ... timeout"
    mbsync-assembly   0
    mbsync-johnw      0
    (all four *-health-check units clean)

Both affected accounts are Gmail-backed and both currently report
result=success -- bia's most recent run at 00:35:04 succeeded, so it self-
recovers exactly as rbcca does. Verbatim shape:
    Socket error on imap.gmail.com (<ip>:993): timeout.
    Synchronization failed with exit code 1

So this is one transient-Gmail-timeout class affecting 2 of 4 accounts at a
combined ~9 failures/10 days, not a per-account fault. John's standing decision
to leave it alone is unchanged; recorded only so a future cycle does not open a
duplicate issue when bia surfaces.

** Close Reason

RETUNED, per John's choice on 2026-08-16 (reversing his 2026-08-11 leave-it-alone).

THE ORIGINAL DIAGNOSIS IN THIS ISSUE WAS RIGHT AND MY FIRST INSTINCT WAS WRONG.
I was about to retune the three Mbsync* alert rules. Measured first: all three
(MbsyncNotRunRecently, MbsyncLastSyncFailed, MbsyncRepeatedFailures) have fired
ZERO times in 14 days. Worst sustained failure is 30 minutes per hour for rbcca
and 15 for bia, never reaching MbsyncRepeatedFailures' 1h clause. Retuning them
would have changed rules that never fire.

The pages come from the GENERIC SystemdServiceFailed rule seeing the unit sit in
'failed' between 15-minute ticks -- exactly as this issue said. Quantified over
14 days:
    mbsync-rbcca.service   254 critical firing samples
    mbsync-bia.service     109 critical firing samples

FIX: retry IN-PROCESS, inside the ExecStart script -- 3 attempts, 60s apart --
so a transient Gmail socket timeout never reaches systemd as a failure at all.
A PERSISTENT failure still exhausts the attempts and fails the unit, so the
detector keeps its meaning; this removes false pages, not coverage.

Retrying in the script rather than via systemd was deliberate, and it sidesteps
both traps this issue documented. Restart = "no" in mkMbsyncModule.nix is
load-bearing (its own comment: restarting blocks nixos-rebuild); 'Restart' in
extraServiceConfig fails Nix evaluation while the base is a plain assignment; and
StartLimitBurst/StartLimitIntervalSec are [Unit] options extraServiceConfig
cannot carry. None of that has to change.

Bounded against the surrounding timings: worst case adds ~2 minutes against a
30min TimeoutStartSec and a 15min timer, and if a retry run overruns the next
tick the existing lock file makes that tick exit 0 rather than pile up.

Verified deployed: the ExecStart script
/nix/store/8rmpq37ya0ybmvf5yicgw6j5yk9sv3bf-mbsync-rbcca contains the retry loop
('for attempt in 1 2 3'), reached via the built unit. All four mbsync units are
currently Result=success.

Note for the next cycle: mbsync is NOT the worst SystemdServiceFailed offender.
pushme-positron.service logged 1955 firing samples over the same 14 days, with
hermes-fallback-counter at 669, logwatch at 644 and git-workspace-archive at 400.
Filed separately.

* DONE [#D] Textfile staleness guard has no coverage backstop: unclassified collectors freeze unnoticed
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-nhd
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-15 Sat 08:50]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-15 Sat 12:48]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-15 Sat 12:48]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

meta_textfile_staleness classifies collectors with three explicit allowlists (fast/hourly/daily, 18 of 51 files). Its comment states that unlisted files are excluded because 'they have their own last-success triads' -- but nothing enforces that assumption.

Found live 2026-08-15: openclaw_canary.prom, residue from the OpenClaw service removed 2026-07-31, had not been written since 2026-08-03 (11.5 days). Its uid/gid (916/908) no longer resolve, no unit or timer writes it, and the only repo reference is a docstring in hermes-self-heal/daemon.py describing what OpenClaw's canary USED to write. Yet node_exporter kept re-serving the file every scrape, so openclaw_gateway_ready_plugins_total read a healthy 6 with a CURRENT timestamp. A frozen instrument presenting as live -- the exact failure class this rule group exists to catch, in the one blind spot it left.

No alert rule or Grafana dashboard consumed any of its 10 metrics, so nothing made a bad decision on it. The risk is the general case, not this instance.

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
IMPLEMENTED and verified in 45a781a7c, plus the live orphan removed.

Two halves, both done.

1. The instance. openclaw_canary.prom -- residue of the OpenClaw service removed 2026-07-31 -- had not been written since 2026-08-03 (11.5 days). Its uid/gid 916/908 no longer resolve, no unit or timer wrote it, and the only repo reference was a docstring in hermes-self-heal/daemon.py describing what OpenClaw USED to write. node_exporter kept re-serving the stale file every scrape, so openclaw_gateway_ready_plugins_total read a healthy 6 carrying a CURRENT timestamp. Verified no rule and no dashboard consumed any of its 10 metrics before removing it; the file was moved aside reversibly, and the phantom series then went 1 -> 0 with node_textfile_scrape_error still 0. Confirmed again this cycle: 0 openclaw_ series, 51 textfiles, 0 scrape errors.

2. The class. TextfileCollectorUnclassifiedStale now backstops the three allowlists, which cover only 18 of 51 files and assume the rest have their own triads -- an assumption nothing enforced.

VERIFIED THIS CYCLE, including the instrument itself rather than just its output:
  - rule loaded in group meta_textfile_staleness, state=inactive, health=ok, for=3600s, lastError=none
  - Prometheus carries 485 rules (was 484), 0 unhealthy, 0 with lastError
  - THE RULE IS ALIVE, NOT MERELY QUIET: running its own expression with the threshold lowered to 0 returns 33 series, proving the negative-match selector and the comparison both work. At the real 14d threshold it returns 0. An inactive rule that could never fire would have returned 0 in both cases -- that is exactly the dead-rule failure this issue is about, so it was worth testing directly.
  - headroom is real: the stalest unclassified file is container_cve.prom at 6.35 days, i.e. 7.65 days below the threshold, so no false positive is imminent
  - the partition is exact: 33 unclassified + 18 classified = 51 total

Threshold reasoning is recorded in the rule comment: the slowest legitimate unlisted cadence is weekly, so 14 days is two consecutive missed weekly runs -- unambiguously dead rather than late -- which follows the same 'detect dead, not skipped' logic the Hourly tier already documents. The negative match means new collectors are covered the moment they appear, and adding a file to a tier above tightens it here automatically.
#+end_example

* DONE [#D] AIDE monitors the obr cache, so routine issue tracking raises AideChangesDetected
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-nox
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-16 Sun 00:52]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-16 Sun 00:53]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-16 Sun 00:53]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

AIDE monitors /etc/nixos/.obr, which is obr's PER-MACHINE CACHE -- a SQLite
database plus history snapshots that obr rewrites on essentially every command.
It self-ignores in git for exactly that reason. The result is that ordinary
issue-tracking activity raises AideChangesDetected.

Measured 2026-08-16. The 00:24 aide-check reported 12 added and 11 changed
entries, and ALL TWELVE additions were .obr cache files:

  /etc/nixos/.obr/history/PLAN.20260816_0615*.org        (6 snapshots)
  /etc/nixos/.obr/history/PLAN.20260816_0615*.org.meta.json  (6 siblings)

with .obr/obr.db, .obr/obr.db-wal, .obr/last-touched and .obr/merge.base.jsonl
among the changed entries. Those timestamps are from a health cycle -- the very
activity that happens several times a day.

This is the same failure the module's own aide-update note warns about, arriving
by a different route: an alert that is permanently on "is no control at all".
The post-rebuild re-baseline cannot help, because the churn is not tied to
rebuilds; it happens whenever obr runs. The database WAS correctly re-baselined
at 22:46:37 by the post-rebuild hook, and aide-check at 00:24 still found
changes, because obr had run in between.

FIX: exclude it, using the same `!` negation convention the file already applies
to .git, result and .nixos-build:

    !/etc/nixos/\.obr

Signal preserved, verified in the same report: the check also correctly flagged
docs/PLAN.org and modules/services/databases.nix, which were genuine tracked
edits. Only the cache is excluded.

** Close Reason

FIXED and verified end to end.

Added `!/etc/nixos/\.obr` to the AIDE rule set, using the same negation convention
the file already applies to .git, result and .nixos-build. Confirmed live in the
generated /etc/aide.conf (line 123, inside the /etc/nixos block).

The exclusion alone was not sufficient, and the intermediate state is worth
recording: the first aide-check after the change reported 182 REMOVED entries,
181 of them .obr paths, because the existing database still contained them and
AIDE no longer scans them. So the alert stayed on for a NEW reason. Re-baselining
with aide-update resolved it, and its ExecStartPost re-measures, so the published
metric agrees with the database that now exists.

Final verdict, from the published textfile:
  aide_check_status 0
  aide_changes_detected 0
  aide_added_files 0
  aide_removed_files 0
  aide_changed_files 0
  aide_total_entries 3468      (was 3660)

And the database itself no longer contains the cache: grep -c '\.obr'
/var/lib/aide/aide.db returns 0.

Signal preserved, not traded away: the same run that exposed this also correctly
flagged docs/PLAN.org and modules/services/databases.nix as changed -- genuine
tracked edits. Only the per-machine cache is excluded.

One observation left for later: the post-rebuild re-baseline hook logged "Started
Post-rebuild AIDE database update" during this switch, but aide.db's mtime did not
move until I ran aide-update by hand. Worth a look if AideChangesDetected latches
again after a future rebuild.

THE BLOCK IS GONE. flake.lock no longer pins 4b977a2f (the version-bump-only tree); it pins ff4d79aa260a, and that revision DOES carry the patch. Counted with the same markers the issue used, and positive-controlled so a zero would have been trustworthy (pushme 101, rsync 136 occurrences):
  SshOptions 19 in 4 files (was 0), CheckRemoteDirectory 6 in 4 (was 0), exitWith 3, RunResult 3, hostSshOptions 2 (all were 0).
pushme-exe-pushme-3.1.0 is already built in the store, so the feared multi-hour ghc-9.10.3 toolchain build has already happened -- that warning in the issue is also stale.

THE SIX REMAINING ITEMS:
 1. Exit codes confirmed FROM SOURCE, Main.hs runResultExitCode: RunUsageError -> 2, TransferError -> 1, TransferWarning and TransferSuccess -> 0. This matches the module's comment exactly, including the deliberate choice to treat a warning as success because it maps to rsync 23/24 (partial transfer, vanished files) which are routine when mirroring a live home directory. NOTE ON MY OWN PROBE: running the binary with a bogus flag exits 1, not 2, but that is optparse-applicative's argument-parse failure, which happens before pushme's RunResult logic; it does not contradict the mapping. The 'verify --help exposes SshOptions/CheckRemoteDirectory' phrasing in the checklist was based on a wrong premise -- both are CONFIG-SCHEMA fields (config.schema.yaml:63-70), not CLI flags, so --help correctly never mentions them.
 2. SshOptions is on the andoria alias in the module.
 3. Destination is /tank/Backups/Contracts/Positron/nfs, not /tank/work/positron.
 4. Timer carries wantedBy = [timers.target] and is active. The 'pair' framing is superseded: restartUnits on the sops secret is deliberately ABSENT, documented with two observed failures -- sops-nix restarting the unit in a separate systemd transaction so the home-manager ordering does not constrain it (a stale destination with rsync --delete), and Type=oneshot blocking switch-to-configuration for up to TimeoutStartSec=12h while holding the build lock.
 5. Log-grep guard replaced by a plain exec. Verified in the DEPLOYED unit script, not just the source: 0 grep-guard occurrences, 1 exec.
 6. Live sync verified: hourly timer, last run 00:04:43 -> 00:16:42 exit 0 Result=success, with '[INFO] andoria done' every hour through the night. Destination exists with 35 top-level entries, mtime 2026-08-22 22:26, 876G under /tank/Backups/Contracts.

No configuration change was needed; the work had been done and simply never verified or closed.
#+end_example

* DONE [#B] hermes-heal could not read the Discord heartbeat, so self-heal could never resolve an incident
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-ots
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-26 Wed 12:59]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-26 Wed 12:59]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-26 Wed 12:59]
:CLOSE_REASON: Fixed and verified end to end; HermesSelfHealStuck no longer firing. Evidence in the description: hermes-heal now carries group 932(hermes), can read the heartbeat, and after the required daemon restart (a switch alone leaves the running process with its original groups) it resolved the latched incident on its first tick -- stuck gauge 1 -> 0 in both the textfile and Prometheus, incident status=resolved, incidents.json written for the first time since 10:41.
:SOURCE_REPO: .
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

FIXED and verified 2026-08-26. Found while triaging a critical HermesSelfHealStuck alert.

SYMPTOM: the self-heal daemon marked an incident stuck and would take no further action. It had correctly SKIPPED its restart_microvm action, noting 'upstream model DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-oQ8e-mtp not serving (gateway HTTP 404); restarting the VM cannot fix an upstream fault' -- good judgement, and a separate bug (see the models.nix fix in b968ad654).

THE REAL DEFECT, found because the incident would not clear even after the upstream was fixed and the VM had restarted: the daemon runs as hermes-heal, which was in NO supplementary groups. probe_clear() -- its ONLY oracle for 'has the agent recovered' -- reads the Discord heartbeat at /var/lib/hermes/.hermes/logs/discord_ws_heartbeat, and every directory on that path is drwxrws--- hermes:hermes. The read therefore raised OSError, _heartbeat_age() returned None, and probe_clear() returned False UNCONDITIONALLY.

CONSEQUENCE, silent and total: no remediation could ever be confirmed, so every incident exhausted its actions and latched as stuck, and reconcile_orphans() could never release one.

HOW LONG: the most recent incident ever marked resolved is 2026-08-05T03:21:39Z -- the same day probe_clear was rewritten to use passive signals. It previously read hermes_mcp_ask_hermes_ok from the health textfile, which hermes-heal CAN read; the rewrite (made to stop synthetic ACK probes polluting John's message history) swapped in a file the daemon had no access to. So self-heal had been unable to close an incident for three weeks while appearing to run normally -- its heartbeat textfile was updating on schedule the whole time.

WHY IT WAS HARD TO SEE: running the daemon's own predicates as root returns probe_clear=True and reconcile_orphans()=1, because root bypasses the permission. The bug only appears when the check runs as hermes-heal.

FIX: extraGroups = [ "hermes" ] on the hermes-heal user in modules/services/hermes-self-heal.nix, mirroring hermes-log-reader in hermes-fallback-counter.nix. Group membership rather than loosening the heartbeat's permissions, per CLAUDE.md.

VERIFIED END TO END: after the switch, id hermes-heal shows 900(hermes-heal),932(hermes) and 'sudo -u hermes-heal test -r' on the heartbeat succeeds. A switch alone was NOT enough -- the running process keeps the groups it started with (/proc/PID/status showed 'Groups: 900'), so the daemon needed a restart; after it, 'Groups: 900 932'. On its very first tick it resolved the incident: hermes_self_heal_stuck_incidents went 1 -> 0 in both the textfile and Prometheus, the incident moved to status=resolved, and incidents.json was written for the first time since 10:41.

#+begin_example
A transient push-mirror failure stays FIRING for hours because nothing retries.
Found 2026-08-19 when GiteaPushMirrorFailing fired for org-agents, the mirror
John had just created.

WHAT HAPPENED, and it was not a broken mirror:
    12:29 PDT  John creates jwiegley/org-agents on GitHub and adds the mirror
    12:32 PDT  Gitea attempts the push; it sets last_error
    15:03 PDT  GiteaPushMirrorFailing starts firing (for: dwell elapsed)
    16:45 PDT  I trigger sync-push-mirrors by hand -> failed=0, alert inactive
Total firing: 414 samples, 22:03-23:46 UTC, about 1h43m.

The content HAD arrived. GitHub's pushed_at was 19:32 UTC and its three most
recent commits (709c0352, d63079e5, aa6b13aa) are byte-identical to Gitea's, so
whatever failed at 12:32 did not stop the mirror working. One manual retry
cleared it.

THE STRUCTURAL PROBLEM. gitea_push_mirror_failed encodes Gitea's `last_error`,
which is LEVEL-TRIGGERED: Gitea sets it on a rejected push and clears it only on
a later SUCCESS. So the flag -- and the alert -- persists until something
retries. And:

,  * sync-push-mirrors HAS NO TIMER. It is a manually-triggered oneshot. Verified:
    `systemctl list-unit-files sync-push-mirrors.timer` finds nothing, and its
    only runs today were 12:23 (before the mirror existed) and my 16:45 trigger.
,  * Gitea's own per-mirror scheduler defaults to an 8 HOUR interval.

So a transient failure can hold this alert for up to 8 hours, and if Gitea's
scheduler also fails once, indefinitely. The alert is truthful about the stored
flag while being stale about reality.

SUGGESTED FIX, not applied: give sync-push-mirrors a timer. The full run over
197 repositories cost 3.76s CPU and finished in seconds, so an hourly or
2-hourly schedule is cheap, and it would make this class of alert self-healing
rather than requiring a human to notice and retry.

NOT DONE AUTONOMOUSLY because it adds periodic outbound pushes to 197 remotes on
a schedule, which is a behaviour change to something that talks to the outside
world, and the cadence is a judgement call.

A CONSTRAINT WORTH RECORDING for whoever debugs the next one: the actual error
text CANNOT be safely read. A Gitea push mirror authenticates with credentials
embedded in remote_address, and last_error quotes that URL back verbatim. The
exporter deliberately emits neither -- only a boolean and a timestamp -- and
that is a deliberate safety property, not an oversight. Diagnose by retrying and
observing the flag, not by fetching the message.

Also corrected while here: gitea_push_mirror_last_update_timestamp_seconds is
the last push ATTEMPT, not the last success. Its own HELP text says so, and I
briefly read it the wrong way.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

Fixed and verified. Cadence chosen by John on 2026-08-19: every 2 hours.

sync-push-mirrors now has a timer -- OnCalendar *-*-0/2:00:00, Persistent=true,
RandomizedDelaySec=10min. Persistent so a retry missed while the host was down
happens at the next boot rather than waiting for the next even hour, which is
exactly the case this is for; the randomised delay keeps 197 outbound pushes off
the hour boundary shared with every other timer here.

This bounds the failure window to ~2h. Before, nothing retried at all: the service
was a manually-triggered oneshot, and Gitea's own per-mirror scheduler defaults to
8 HOURS, so a transient failure held GiteaPushMirrorFailing until a human noticed.
On 2026-08-19 that meant 1h43m of firing over a mirror whose content had in fact
reached GitHub eleven minutes after it was created.

Cost is measured rather than assumed: a full run over 197 repositories took 3.76s
of CPU and finished in seconds.

VERIFIED after /etc/nixos/build switch: sync-push-mirrors.timer is present and
enabled, Persistent=yes, RandomizedDelayUSec=10min.

#+begin_example
Five separate monitoring/housekeeping gaps found by the 2026-08-20 sweep, all INFO,
all adversarially verified. Grouped because each is small and they share a theme:
things that stopped being watched after a service was removed, or were never watched.


### [info] dmesg ring buffer retains only ~34 minutes because ForwardToKMsg=yes floods it with userspace journal records

EVIDENCE: The kernel ring buffer spans only 10:02:05 to 10:35:51 (~34 min, 627 lines, 134,920 bytes, kernel-default size with no log_buf_len on /proc/cmdline). The content is overwhelmingly userspace: `dmesg -x` shows facility `user:notice` (92) and `user:err` (86), and line prefixes are podman/sudo/redis/matter-server/openproject — only 1 line is an audit record, so this is not audit noise. Cause is ForwardToKMsg=yes at /etc/nixos/modules/core/crash-debug.nix:78, whose comment reads 'Forward kernel messages to kmsg for dmesg visibility' — that is inverted: ForwardToKMsg pushes journal (userspace) records INTO kmsg; kernel messages already originate there. Net effect is the opposite of the stated intent — zero usb/scsi/uas/xhci lines survive in the ring buffer. This matters on a host with a documented USB/UAS enclosure bridge-hang failure mode, where `dmesg` is the first-reflex tool and the diagno

COMMAND: sudo dmesg -T | head -1; sudo dmesg -T | tail -1; sudo dmesg -x | grep -oE '^[a-z]+ +:[a-z]+' | sort | uniq -c; grep -rn ForwardToKMsg /etc/nixos

### [info] /var/lib/containers/litellm holds 3.0G under an unallocated UID with no user manager left to prune it, contradicting the documented "prune timer handles them" assumption

EVIDENCE: `getent passwd litellm` -> NO SUCH USER; `getent passwd 948` and `getent group 939` both unallocated; `find /var/lib/containers -maxdepth 1 -type d -printf '%u:%g %f\n'` prints "948:939 litellm" while every other entry resolves to a real name; `stat -c '%U:%G'` -> UNKNOWN:UNKNOWN. `du -sh /var/lib/containers/litellm` = 3.0G of 30G total. There is no linger entry for litellm and 0 litellm systemd units remain, so the cleanup was otherwise thorough. The gap is structural: podman-image-prune is a `systemd --user` timer inside the user's own manager (modules/users/home-manager/rootless-podman-image-prune.nix), so once the account and its linger are gone the timer can never run again and that 3.0G is unreclaimable by any scheduled job. The residue memory explicitly files "Dangling podman images — a weekly home-manager prune timer handles them" under "Apparent residue that is HARMLESS — do not

COMMAND: getent passwd litellm; getent passwd 948; getent group 939; sudo find /var/lib/containers -maxdepth 1 -mindepth 1 -type d -printf '%u:%g %f\n' | sort; sudo du -sh /var/lib/containers/litellm

### [info] container_image_local_age_days has never emitted a single sample — podman's .Created format is unparseable by date -d, so image-age monitoring is silently blind

EVIDENCE: The staleness exporter declares the gauge (container-image-staleness-exporter.nix:154-155 HELP/TYPE, :209 the emit) but /var/lib/prometheus-node-exporter-textfiles/container_image_staleness.prom contains exactly 2 matching lines — the HELP and TYPE headers — and ZERO sample lines (`grep -m5 '^container_image_local_age_days'` → no match). count(container_image_local_age_days) = 0 now and count(count_over_time(...[30d])) = 0, and the metric name is absent from Prometheus's __name__ index entirely (while litellm_* names dead since 2026-08-01 are still indexed), so it has never been written. Root cause proven: `podman image inspect ghcr.io/home-assistant-libs/python-matter-server:stable --format '{{.Created}}'` returns `2025-08-14 08:01:41.197445042 +0000 UTC`, and `date -d` on that string exits 1 (GNU date cannot take a trailing ' UTC' after an explicit '+0000'), so the `if [[ -n "$created_

COMMAND: C=$(sudo podman image inspect ghcr.io/home-assistant-libs/python-matter-server:stable --format '{{.Created}}'); echo "[$C]"; date -d "$C" +%s; echo rc=$?; date -d "2026-07-15T09:22:31Z" +%s; sudo grep -c container_image_

### [info] Orphaned 2.73 GiB podman store for the removed litellm user, unpruned and still publishing a phantom metric 19 days after service removal

EVIDENCE: /var/lib/containers/litellm is 2,932,285,153 bytes (2.73 GiB) and `ls -la /var/lib/containers/` shows it owned by unresolved numeric uid 948 / gid 939 (every other store resolves to a live name); `getent passwd litellm` returns nothing. Its mtime is Aug 1, matching the 2026-08-01 LiteLLM removal. container_store_size_bytes{user="litellm"} is still live at 2.73 GiB, and query_range over 30d shows it climbing 1.094 → 2.787 GiB through 08-01 then flat at 2.731 GiB ever since — i.e. the exporter's runtime glob over /var/lib/containers keeps reporting disk for a service that no longer exists. Nothing will reclaim it: the weekly prune is a home-manager systemd --user timer installed per rootless user (rootless-podman-image-prune.nix:38 keys on home.homeDirectory), so deleting the user deleted its pruner along with it. Linger was correctly cleaned (`ls /var/lib/systemd/linger/` has no litellm),

COMMAND: sudo du -sb /var/lib/containers/litellm; sudo ls -la /var/lib/containers/ | grep litellm; getent passwd litellm; curl -s http://localhost:9090/api/v1/query --data-urlencode 'query=container_store_size_bytes{user="litellm

### [info] ContainerPruneStale's documented absent() arm does not exist, so a rootless prune timer that stops emitting is unmonitored

EVIDENCE: container-health.yaml:80-84 comments that container_image_prune_last_trigger_seconds 'is only emitted for users whose timer exists AND has fired at least once, so a never-fired timer shows up as absent — caught by the absent() arm below', but `grep -n 'absent' modules/monitoring/alerts/container-health.yaml` returns exactly one line — line 82, the comment itself. There is no absent() rule anywhere in the file. The single ContainerPruneStale expr is `time() - container_image_prune_last_trigger_seconds > 9*24*3600`, which by construction cannot fire on a series that has vanished. This is not hypothetical coverage loss: the metric currently has 10 series while /var/lib/containers holds 12 non-root stores, and the litellm case above is a live example of a store whose prune series disappeared with zero alerting. The remaining gap-check, ContainerStoreSizeHigh, only trips at 60 GiB, so a store

COMMAND: grep -n "absent\|PruneStale\|prune_last_trigger" /etc/nixos/modules/monitoring/alerts/container-health.yaml; grep -rc "absent(" /etc/nixos/modules/monitoring/alerts/*.yaml | grep -v ':0'

### [info] redis-grist is the only Redis instance with zero monitoring coverage: absent from both the redis-multi scrape targets and the systemd backstop alert

EVIDENCE: 7 Redis units are active (gitea, grist, immich, openproject, rspamd, searxng, speedtest-tracker). Prometheus `count(redis_up)` returns 7 series, but the distinct instances are redis://127.0.0.1:{6381,6383,6386,6387}, unix:///run/redis-{gitea,immich}/redis.sock, and job="redis" localhost:9121 (which duplicates rspamd via -redis.addr). There is no series for grist on 6388, even though `ss -tlnp` shows 0.0.0.0:6388 LISTEN and the unit is active. modules/monitoring/services/redis-exporter.nix lists six redis-multi targets and its own comment claims 'All five TCP-listening instances are reachable on 127.0.0.1' while enumerating only four (6383, 6386, 6381, 6387) — grist (6388, declared in modules/containers/grist-quadlet.nix:137) is the missed fifth. The documented backstop is also scoped past it: alerts/redis.yaml:152 matches `redis-(gitea|immich)\.service` only, and no alert file mentions g

COMMAND: curl -s http://127.0.0.1:9090/api/v1/query?query=redis_up; /run/current-system/sw/bin/ss -tlnp | grep -E ':6388|:6381|:6383|:6386|:6387'; grep -rn 'grist' /etc/nixos/modules/monitoring/alerts/*.yaml; curl -s 'http://127.


NONE FIXED THIS CYCLE -- scope control, not difficulty. The cycle already shipped the
timer fix, the auth.log rotation/permission fix and the ZFS snapshot-depth monitoring,
and each of these deserves its own verification rather than being swept in.

The two litellm items are the same root cause seen twice (an orphaned per-user podman
store and home directory left behind by the 2026-08-01 service removal, ~5.7G total,
under a UID with no user manager left to prune it). The image-age collector emitting
NOTHING is the most interesting of the five: podman's .Created format is unparseable
by `date -d`, so container_image_local_age_days has never produced a single sample and
image-age monitoring has been silently blind since it was written.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

All five closed. Four fixed in config, one resolved by deletion at John's explicit
direction. Each verified empirically rather than by rebuild-succeeded.

1. ForwardToKMsg=yes -> no (crash-debug.nix). The setting was commented "Forward
   kernel messages to kmsg for dmesg visibility", which is backwards: it pushes
   JOURNAL records INTO the ring buffer, and kernel messages already originate
   there. Before: dmesg spanned ~34 minutes with facility user:notice 92 / user:err
   86 and ZERO usb/scsi/uas/xhci lines -- on a host whose documented failure mode is
   a USB/UAS enclosure bridge hang where dmesg is the first-reflex tool.
   VERIFIED by injecting a userspace record with `logger` and confirming it does NOT
   reach kmsg (0 hits, dmesg line count unchanged at 702). Pre-existing userspace
   lines remain until the fixed-size buffer cycles them out; kernel messages are
   still journalled (journalctl -k) and ForwardToSyslog is untouched.

2+3. /var/lib/containers/litellm DELETED -- 2.73 GiB, 60,468 files, owned by
   unresolvable uid 948:gid 939. John authorised this explicitly. Pre-flight checked
   first, and the mount check is the one that mattered: no litellm user, uid 948
   unallocated, 0 linger entries, 0 systemd units, 0 mount entries under the path,
   no open file handles (lsof), no repo references. /var went 27% -> 26% and every
   remaining container store now resolves to a real user -- no UNKNOWN:UNKNOWN.
   The phantom metric cleared too: container_store_size.prom went from 13 series
   including litellm to 12 with zero litellm lines.

4. container_image_local_age_days had NEVER emitted a sample. podman renders
   .Created as Go's native time.Time string ("2025-08-14 08:01:41.197445042 +0000
   UTC") and GNU date refuses a trailing " UTC" after an explicit "+0000", so the
   guard never passed. Fixed by asking podman for RFC3339 via
   {{.Created.Format "2006-01-02T15:04:05Z07:00"}} rather than stripping the suffix
   in shell, so it survives podman changing its rendering again.
   VERIFIED: 0 sample lines before, 15 after. matter-server reads 371 days and
   wallabag 317 -- real drift the metric existed to show and never did.
   Positive-controlled first against every image the exporter actually inspects
   (it enumerates running containers, so dangling <none> images never reach it):
   11/11 parsed.

5. ContainerPruneMissing added -- the absent arm the comment had promised for who
   knows how long. Grepping the file for "absent" matched exactly one line: the
   comment claiming the rule existed. ContainerPruneStale is a time()-since-last-fire
   test and cannot fire on a series that never appeared.
   NOT literal absent(), which returns 1 only when a selector matches nothing at all
   and so cannot find one missing user among ten; the set difference is the right
   operator.
   SCOPED ON container_running, NOT container_store_size_bytes -- I tried the store
   metric first and it produced two FALSE POSITIVES: `root` (pruned by
   virtualisation.podman.autoPrune, not a per-user timer) and
   `technitium-dns-exporter`, which has a /var/lib/containers home but runs its
   container as a SYSTEM container per its own module header, its directory being
   376K of home rather than a populated overlay. Keying on
   container_running{user!="root"} asks the question that matters.
   VERIFIED: scans 10 rootless users, 0 gaps, health=ok. Preventive by design --
   it catches the documented CONTAINER_USERS/ROOTLESS_USERS drift, which is exactly
   how litellm's pruner vanished with its account and left 2.73 GiB behind.

6. redis-grist (6388) added to the redis-multi targets. MY OWN GAP: I declared that
   instance in grist-quadlet.nix on 2026-08-19 and never added it here, leaving it
   the only Redis on the host with no coverage at all -- absent from these probes
   AND from the systemd backstop, which matches only redis-(gitea|immich).
   VERIFIED: redis_up went from 7 series to 8, with redis://127.0.0.1:6388 at up=1.
   Replaced the header's stale claim ("all five TCP-listening instances" while
   listing four) with the command that re-derives coverage instead.

#+begin_example
### Postfix serves a TLS cert expiring in 34 days; postfix-cert-renewal has been renewing the wrong directory for ~11 months while reporting success

EVIDENCE: Postfix reads its chain from /var/lib/postfix-certs/smtp.vulcan.lan.fullchain.crt (postconf -h smtpd_tls_chain_files). Those files have mtime Sep 23 2025 and are owned johnw:users, not root. Live on the wire, ports 25, 587 and 465 all present CN=smtp.vulcan.lan with notAfter=Sep 23 19:32:38 2026 -- 34 days out; Prometheus agrees (certificate_days_until_expiry{name="smtp.vulcan.lan"}=34). Meanwhile postfix-cert-renewal.service (Result=success, ExecMainStatus=0, last triggered Sat 2026-08-01 04:11:14) execs /etc/nixos/certs/postfix-cert-renew.sh, whose POSTFIX_CERT_DIR="/etc/postfix/certs" -- a different directory. /etc/postfix/certs/{mail,smtp,imap}.crt are indeed fresh (mtime Aug 1 04:11) but nothing references them. The script has no expiry threshold guard, so it renews unconditionally into the orphan path every month. Dovecot is the control: dovecot-cert-renew.sh sets DOVECOT_CERT_DIR="/var/lib/dovecot-certs", those files updated Aug 1 04:38, and ports 993/143 serve notAfter=Aug 1 2027. Next postfix timer run is Sep 1 and will again miss; the cert dies Sep 23, taking submission/smtps TLS with it.

COMMAND: sudo postconf -h smtpd_tls_chain_files; sudo ls -la /var/lib/postfix-certs/; grep -nE 'CERT_DIR=' /etc/nixos/certs/postfix-cert-renew.sh; echo | timeout 8 openssl s_client -connect 127.0.0.1:587 -starttls smtp 2>/dev/null | openssl x509 -noout -subject -enddate

VERIFIER: Fully reproduced with four independent instruments that agree, and it survives all four refutation questions.

INSTRUMENT SOUNDNESS (Q1): Not an artifact. (a) `sudo postconf -n | grep -iE 'certs|\.crt'` shows the ONLY cert path in postfix's live config is `smtpd_tls_chain_files = /var/lib/postfix-certs/smtp.vulcan.lan.{key,fullchain.crt}`; `/etc/postfix/certs` appears nowhere in the running config (smtp_tls_CAfile is the nss-cacert store path). (b) On disk, /var/lib/postfix-certs/* are all mtime Sep 23 2025 owned johnw:users, and `openssl x509` gives subject=CN=smtp.vulcan.lan, notAfter=Sep 23 19:32:38 2026. /etc/postfix/certs/smtp.crt is mtime Aug 1 04:11 with subject=CN=smtp.vulcan.lan, O=Vulcan Mail Services, notAfter=Aug 1 11:11:14 2027. (c) On the wire, ports 25, 587 and 465 each return subject=CN=smtp.vulcan.lan with NO O= component and notAfter=Sep 23 2026 -- the differing subject DN between the two files is a discriminating fingerprint, so the wire is unambiguously serving the 2025 file, not the Aug 2026 one. (d) Prometheus: certificate_days_until_expiry{name="smtp.vulcan.lan", path="/var/lib/postfix-certs/smtp.vulcan.lan.crt"} = 34. No journal retention, TSDB-choice, regex

### The bare vulcan.lan vhost certificate expires in 33 days and no unit or timer renews it

EVIDENCE: /var/lib/nginx-certs/vulcan.lan.crt expires Sep 23 10:03:26 2026 (33 days; certificate_days_until_expiry{name="vulcan.lan"}=33) and backs a live TLS vhost -- the generated nginx.conf has server_name vulcan.lan vulcan on listen 443 ssl with ssl_certificate /var/lib/nginx-certs/vulcan.lan.crt, and openssl s_client -servername vulcan.lan returns subject=CN=vulcan.lan. The renewal script's DOMAINS array does not contain it, and its own header states vulcan.lan is not renewed there and has no automatic renewal. A repo-wide grep for a renewer found only modules/services/web.nix (the vhost that consumes it) and CERTIFICATES.md; the certificates.nix hit is step-ca's own dnsNames list, not a renewer. Two other live vhosts share this class with more runway: changes.vulcan.lan (71 days) and mailarchiver.vulcan.lan (87 days), both with real server_name blocks and no renewer. The generic CertificateExpiringSoon rule (<=30 days) will start firing for vulcan.lan in about 3 days, but firing does not fix it since no automation exists to renew it.

COMMAND: cd /var/lib/nginx-certs && for f in *.crt; do end=$(sudo openssl x509 -in "$f" -noout -enddate | cut -d= -f2); echo $(( ($(date -d "$end" +%s)-$(date +%s))/86400 )) "$f"; done | sort -n | head; sed -n '1,20p' /etc/nixos/certs/renew-nginx-certs.sh

VERIFIER: REPRODUCED — could not refute. Evidence is direct openssl, immune to this host's documented instrument traps (no journal retention, no TSDB, no post-switch reset).

1. INSTRUMENT SOUND. openssl x509 -enddate on /var/lib/nginx-certs/vulcan.lan.crt => Sep 23 10:03:26 2026 GMT = 33 days. openssl s_client -connect 127.0.0.1:443 -servername vulcan.lan => subject=CN=vulcan.lan, same notAfter, so it backs a LIVE vhost. Generated nginx.conf (/nix/store/bg5dy1h0iab4nm12fn59zyk5fjcyp9s3-nginx.conf:1337-1340) has server_name vulcan.lan vulcan on listen 443 ssl with that cert.

NEGATIVE CLAIM POSITIVE-CONTROLLED (the part I tried hardest to break):
 - nginx-cert-renewal.timer (OnCalendar=*-*-01 03:30) -> nginx-cert-renewal.service -> store wrapper that literally execs /etc/nixos/certs/renew-nginx-certs.sh. So the deployed unit runs the EXACT repo file read; no "store copy has a different DOMAINS list" escape hatch. That array has no vulcan.lan.
 - systemctl list-unit-files | grep -i cert shows seven per-service *-certificate.service units (atd, budgetboard, glances, grafana, qdrant, speedtest-tracker, victoriametrics); none for the bare host.
 - Grep positive control: grep for grafana.vulcan.l


BOTH NEED JOHN: certificate issuance is explicitly his call on this host -- the
standing instruction is to ask rather than generate. I have not run
renew-certificate.sh and will not. The renewal AUTOMATION defect (postfix renewing
the wrong directory while reporting success) is fixable from the repo once John has
decided how he wants the certs issued.

DEADLINES ARE REAL AND CLOSE: 33 and 34 days from 2026-08-20.

,** Notes

2026-08-20: automation FIXED and committed (113cdb0a6). John's decision: do NOT
issue certificates now; let the 2026-09-01 timers do it. Deferred to 2026-09-02 to
verify the outcome rather than assume it.

WHY WAITING IS SAFE -- checked, not assumed. The old script could not fail loudly:
it wrote to an orphan directory and exited 0 every time, for ~11 months. The rewrite
inverts that. It now (a) refuses to start if `postconf -h smtpd_tls_chain_files`
disagrees with its target directory, and (b) after reloading, compares the on-disk
notAfter against what port 587 actually presents, exiting nonzero on mismatch.

A nonzero exit fails postfix-cert-renewal.service, and that unit IS visible to the
alerting: node_systemd_unit_state{name="postfix-cert-renewal.service",state="failed"}
exists as a live series (value 0 today), and SystemdServiceFailed selects
{state="failed",name=~".*\\.service"} with for:1m. Same for nginx-cert-renewal.
So a bad Sep 1 run pages within a minute, with 22 days of runway before the Sep 23
expiries. That is the whole basis for deferring rather than forcing it now.

VERIFY ON OR AFTER 2026-09-02:
  # postfix -- the served cert must move from Sep 23 2026 to ~Sep 2027
  echo | openssl s_client -connect 127.0.0.1:587 -starttls smtp 2>/dev/null \
    | openssl x509 -noout -subject -enddate
  # the monitored leaf must move WITH it (this is the half that could silently lag)
  sudo openssl x509 -in /var/lib/postfix-certs/smtp.vulcan.lan.crt -noout -enddate
  # the four newly-covered nginx vhosts
  curl -s --data-urlencode 'query=certificate_days_until_expiry{name=~"vulcan.lan|changes.vulcan.lan|mailarchiver.vulcan.lan|chat.vulcan.lan"}' \
    http://127.0.0.1:9090/api/v1/query
  systemctl show postfix-cert-renewal.service -p Result --value   # expect success
  systemctl show nginx-cert-renewal.service  -p Result --value    # expect success

WHAT WOULD MEAN IT IS STILL WRONG: the wire still reading Sep 23 2026, OR the wire
updating while smtp.vulcan.lan.crt does not. The second is the subtle one -- postfix
serves *.fullchain.crt while certificate-exporter.nix reads *.crt, and
renew-certificate.sh writes only one cert file, so a naive fix updates the served
cert and leaves the MONITORED one stale. The script extracts the leaf explicitly to
prevent that; confirm both dates match.

TWO THINGS FOUND AND DELIBERATELY LEFT (not part of this issue, no deadline):
,,  * atd.vulcan.lan (89d) and budget.vulcan.lan (71d) are renewed by their own
,,    *-certificate units, which are activation-time oneshots with RemainAfterExit --
    both last ran at the 2026-07-03 boot. They renew on REBOOT, not on a schedule.
,,  * Five certs persist with no live vhost (llama-swap, copyparty, notebook,
    syncthing, openclaw), and teable is the mirror image: still in DOMAINS and
    renewed monthly with nothing serving it.

* DONE [#C] Changing sockpuppetbrowser.service silently takes changedetection down and leaves it down
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-rqc
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-27 Thu 04:46]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-27 Thu 15:49]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:FINISHED: [2026-08-27 Thu 15:49]
:SOURCE_REPO: .
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

CAUSED BY ME on 2026-08-27, and it cost a ~4 hour outage of a monitored service. Immediate problem fixed; the underlying trap is NOT fixed and will recur.

WHAT HAPPENED: I deployed a SuccessExitStatus fix to sockpuppetbrowser.service (commit 9cc464423). Because that changed the unit, the home-manager activation during ./build switch restarted it. changedetection.service has Requires=sockpuppetbrowser.service, so it was stopped as a dependency casualty at 00:46:14. sockpuppetbrowser came back at 00:47:55. changedetection did not, and stayed down until I started it at 04:44:14 -- 3h58m, during which ChangeDetectionAppDown, ChangeDetectionHTTPSProbeFailed and WebServiceDown were all firing CRITICAL.

WHY IT DID NOT COME BACK, confirmed from the live unit rather than assumed:
  Restart=always          -- only covers UNEXPECTED process exits, not a deliberate stop
  WantedBy=default.target -- only starts it when the target is activated
  UpheldBy=               -- empty, so nothing continuously ensures it runs
A dependency-cascade stop is a deliberate stop. Nothing in that set restarts one.

SO THE TRAP IS GENERAL: any future edit to sockpuppetbrowser.service takes changedetection down and leaves it down, silently, until something notices. It is not specific to my change.

MY PROCESS FAILURE, which is the more useful lesson: after the switch I verified the unit I had CHANGED (sockpuppetbrowser: active/running, SuccessExitStatus=143 applied, SIGTERM path now clean) and never checked the unit that DEPENDS on it. Verifying the thing you touched is not the same as verifying the blast radius of touching it. The alerts caught it, but only on the next 4-hourly sweep.

CANDIDATE FIX (not applied, deliberately): Upholds= on sockpuppetbrowser pointing at changedetection.service. systemd 254+ supports it and it exists for exactly this -- "while A is active, keep B active". Deploying it is itself another edit to sockpuppetbrowser.service, which would again stop changedetection during activation; with Upholds in place systemd should bring it straight back, so the deploy doubles as the test. I did not do this at 05:00 unattended, having already caused one outage on these units tonight.

ALTERNATIVES WORTH WEIGHING: relax Requires= to Wants= so changedetection is never stopped by the browser cycling (weaker ordering guarantee, but changedetection tolerates the browser being briefly absent); or accept the coupling and add a post-switch check. Upholds looks cleanest but the choice is John's.

INTERIM MITIGATION: after any switch that touches modules/users/home-manager/changedetection.nix, check
  systemctl --user -M changedetection@ status changedetection.service
or simply confirm https://changes.vulcan.lan returns 200.

** Close Reason

FIXED and proven 2026-08-27. Requires= -> Wants= on changedetection.service in modules/users/home-manager/changedetection.nix, keeping After= so boot ordering is unchanged.

WHY Wants= IS THE RIGHT STRENGTH, not a weakening: the browser is not a startup dependency. changedetection reaches it per-fetch over PLAYWRIGHT_DRIVER_URL=ws://10.0.2.2:3008, so it starts and serves its UI with the browser absent; only JS-rendered fetches fail until it returns, and those retry on schedule. Requires= was buying stop-propagation nobody wanted.

PROVEN, not inferred. Restarting sockpuppetbrowser -- the exact action that caused the 3h58m outage this morning -- left changedetection's ActiveEnterTimestamp UNCHANGED at 15:44:04 while the browser cycled to 15:44:20, with https://changes.vulcan.lan returning 200 throughout. Before the fix that same action stopped changedetection and left it stopped.

Also verified the deploy itself was safe: only changedetection.service changed, so home-manager restarted it alone and did not touch sockpuppetbrowser (still up since 00:47:55) -- no cascade during activation. And this time I checked BOTH units after the switch, which is the step whose omission caused the outage.

Upholds= (systemd 258 supports it) was considered and rejected: it would keep Requires= and auto-restart the casualty, papering over the cascade instead of preventing it, and would make changedetection impossible to stop for maintenance while the browser runs.

* DEFER [#E] Headscale for John's own private network (replacing WireGuard) — DEFERRED
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-rqw
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: feature
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 16:39]
:MODIFIED: [2026-08-11 Tue 22:25]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

DEFERRED — planned future work, not active.

CORRECTED PREMISE (John, 2026-08-11): vulcan is NOT and will never be intended to join the
Tailscale network that andoria-08 sits on. Any earlier framing of this issue as 'join the tailnet
so pushme can reach andoria directly' was wrong; pushme reaches andoria-08 through the hera SSH
jump and that is the intended design.

WHAT THIS ISSUE ACTUALLY IS: Headscale as a self-hosted coordination plane for John's OWN private
network, replacing the WireGuard setup currently in use. The accepted design and full deployment
runbook already exist at docs/TAILSCALE_HEADSCALE_PLAN.md — 'design accepted; not yet implemented',
re-verified 2026-07-27. Chosen architecture is Headscale on vulcan with OPNsense as the network's
entry point, in phases 0-7.

PREREQUISITE recorded from nixos-cmq: the firewall must default-deny on unknown interfaces before a
new interface (headscale/wireguard) appears, otherwise the 17 TCP + 6 UDP globally-declared ports
become reachable from it the moment it exists.

Do not work this autonomously. It spans vulcan and OPNsense, stands up a new coordination server,
and replaces working WireGuard connectivity — John should be present.

* DEFER [#D] Matter node 17 (Thread door sensor): recurring CASE-failure episodes, Aug 16 and Aug 19
:PROPERTIES:
:OBR_SCHEMA_VERSION: 17
:ID:       nixos-rsn
:LABELS:   []
:ISSUE_TYPE: bug
:CREATED:  [2026-08-16 Sun 04:46]
:MODIFIED: [2026-09-01 Tue 12:45]
:CREATED_BY: johnw
:DEFERRED: [2026-11-18 Wed 09:00]
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos
:SOURCE_REPO_PATH: /etc/nixos
:ORIGINAL_SIZE: 0
:END:

A SECOND Matter node began failing on 2026-08-15, distinct from the outdoor plug
in nixos-h2u, and it is ESCALATING.

  peer <0000000000000011  =  node 17  =  Aqara Door and Window Sensor P2
                             (serial ends 96BDBE; the nodes carry no labels)

Failures per hour, from the matter-server journal:
  08-15 22:00   1
  08-15 23:00   6
  08-16 00:00  12
  08-16 01:00  18
plus a cluster of 12 on 08-14 13:00. Monotonic ramp, not a flat background.

IT IS NOT THE SAME FAULT AS nixos-h2u. Node 26 (the outdoor plug) is WiFi,
FeatureMap=1. Node 17 is THREAD, FeatureMap=2, so the Aqara Hub M3 border router
IS in its path -- the opposite of the plug, where I explicitly ruled the hub out.

IT IS ALSO NOT THE BORDER-ROUTER SPOF, on the evidence. The other Thread nodes
are essentially clean over the same 48h:
  node  6 (Thread, P2 sensor)      1 failure
  node 18 (Thread, P2 sensor)      0 failures
  node 25 (Thread, FP300 presence) 0 failures
  node 24 (the Hub M3 itself, Ethernet) 10 failures
A failing border router would take its children down together; three of four
Thread children are fine. So this points at node 17 specifically -- its link to
the mesh, its position, or the device.

BATTERY IS RULED OUT. All three Aqara P2 door sensors report 100% in Home
Assistant (office, master_bedroom, tv). The lowest battery on the whole fleet is
sensor.front_door_battery_2 at 20%, which is a different device and not a Matter
Thread node.

Accompanying symptoms that appeared with it, ~22 each in 4h:
  CHIP_ERROR [chip.native.DIS] Timeout waiting for mDNS resolution
  CHIP_ERROR [chip.native.DIS] OperationalSessionSetup: operational discovery failed
  WARNING [matter_server.server.device_controller] Setup for node failed
so the failure is at discovery/session setup, not mid-session.

FUNCTIONALLY STILL WORKING, as far as HA can tell: all three P2 sensors still
report, and none is 'unavailable'. Like the plug, this is currently noise plus
latency rather than a dead device -- but unlike the plug it is getting worse.

NOT ACTIONABLE FROM HERE, same reasons as nixos-h2u: the next steps are physical
(which room is serial ...96BDBE, is it newly obstructed, has anything moved or
changed near it since 08-14) and RF-side (Thread mesh routing and neighbour
quality around the Hub M3). Do NOT restart matter-server -- prior experience on
this host is that it does not help and the 2026-08-10 storm began at a restart.

Mapping node 17 to a room needs HA's device registry under /var/lib/hass/.storage,
which is on the forbidden-read list, so John will need to match the serial in the
HA UI (Settings -> Devices -> Matter).

** Notes

2026-08-19 RE-MEASUREMENT: "quiet since" IS NO LONGER TRUE. There is a second,
LARGER episode today.

Node 17 (peer <0000000000000011, Aqara Door and Window Sensor P2, Thread):

  08-14  14      08-17   0
  08-15   7      08-18   3
  08-16  30      08-19  51   <- today, 70% larger than the Aug 16 episode

All 51 of today's lines are the same signature as before: "CASESession timed out
while...". Not a new fault class.

SHAPE: light morning scatter then a sustained evening block.
  06:00  3    07:00  5    09:00  1
  18:00  9    19:00 12    20:00 10    21:00 11
Last event 21:36:44; quiet for 2h19m at time of writing. So this reads as episodic
in the same way Aug 16 did, not as a permanent degradation.

The 18:00-21:00 block is NOT explained by my rebuilds. It starts at 18:00, well
before the 19:22:50 build that restarted HA and PostgreSQL, and continues past it
to 21:36.

THE DEVICE IS NOT GONE, which was the first thing worth ruling out because a quiet
node and a dead node look identical in the log: binary_sensor.front_door_door is
present in HA's recorder with state=off, last written 19:26 (the HA restart). It
does not read unavailable. Door sensors only write on change, so no rows since
midnight is expected, not suspicious.

STILL NOT ACTING, and this is why: node 17 is Thread, and per the fabric topology
the Aqara Hub M3 is the only Thread border router on this fabric -- a single point
of failure whose remedy is physical. Restarting matter-server is explicitly the
wrong move. Two episodes three days apart, both self-clearing, with the device
still reporting, is evidence for a flaky border-router path rather than anything
fixable from this repo.

FOR JOHN: if a third episode lands, the border router is the thing to look at
physically -- placement, power, or interference -- not the sensor and not the
server. Worth knowing that node 26 (the outdoor plug, nixos-h2u) went the OTHER
way over the same window, collapsing from ~1900/day to single digits, so whatever
changed on the fabric did not affect both nodes the same way. They are different
transports: node 26 is WiFi, node 17 is Thread.

INSTRUMENT: I re-hit the exact trap this issue warns about. The real format is
`peer <0000000000000011` with NO closing bracket; my pattern required one and
returned zero for every node on every day. Positive-control against a known-busy
day before trusting a zero.

    "text": "2026-08-19 re-measurement: still quiet. Four failure lines in the whole of the last\neight days, all on day -4 (2026-08-15/16, the known episode). Days -1, -2, -3 and\n-5 through -8 are all ZERO. Nothing new since.\n\nA NOTE ON THE INSTRUMENT, because I got this wrong three times before getting it\nright, and the wrong answers were each plausible:\n\n  1. `grep -ciE 'node.*17.*(fail|unreach)'` reported 44 hits in 24 hours and 2639\n     in 7 days -- which would have read as an ongoing, worsening problem. Every one\n     was a substring artifact: `.*17.*` matches 117, 170, timestamps, session ids.\n  2. Tightening to `\\bnode[ _]?17\\b|<17>` returned ZERO for all seven days --\n     including the day of a KNOWN episode. Zero on a day something demonstrably\n     happened is not a clean result, it is a broken instrument, and that is what\n     stopped me reporting \"resolved\".\n  3. The reason is that matter-server writes node ids as zero-padded HEX:\n     `Node: <0000000000000011>`. Node 17 decimal is 0x11. A decimal pattern cannot\n     match it, and `Node: <[0-9]+>` fails too because the field is 16 digits wide.\n\nThe correct pattern is `Node: <0*11\\b`. Anyone re-checking this issue should use it;\nsearching for \"17\" in these logs is meaningless in either direction.\n\nIncidental, from the same extraction: node 0x00 accounts for 2502 of the ~2536 node\nreferences in 7 days, so the overwhelming majority of matter-server's error lines\ncarry no real node id at all. That is relevant to nixos-mqk -- most of that volume\nis not attributable to any specific device.\n",

The devices are healthy and reachable from the\nhub's side. The fault is specific to Home Assistant's path to them.\n\nTHE CLEAN DISCRIMINATOR, measured from mDNS:\n  reached over IPv4          node 24 (hub), node 26 (plug)   -> WORK\n  reached over Thread mesh   nodes 6, 17, 18, 25             -> ALL FAIL\nEvery device HA reaches by IPv4 works. :CLOSE_REASON: Verified and re-baselined. The drift was exactly my 5 retry nodes — proven by the baseline sha256 matching my pre-edit backup byte-for-byte — and config-drift-rebaseline.service has approved it. All 7 tracked files read drift=0 and the rule is inactive.
:SOURCE_REPO: nixos

#+begin_example
ConfigFileChangedOutsideDeploy fired on flows.json. TRUE POSITIVE, and the change
was MINE: I hand-edited /var/lib/node-red/flows.json to add the Nest-mirror retry
nodes (nixos-ii9) and restarted node-red, rather than deploying through Node-RED.

WHY IT FIRED, exactly as the rule's own runbook predicts: a legitimate Node-RED
deploy advances .flows.json.backup in lockstep and re-baselines silently. Editing
the file directly bypasses that path, so the backup stayed at its 2026-08-19 12:54
state while flows.json moved to 2026-08-20 15:25. The detector did its job.

I could not have used the sanctioned path: Node-RED's adminAuth reads its bearer
token from /run/secrets/node-red/api-tokens, and reading anything under
/run/secrets is forbidden on this host. So a direct edit plus a restart was the
only route available, and drift firing afterwards is the expected consequence.

VERIFIED BEFORE APPROVING, because re-baselining approves whatever is on disk and
a blind re-baseline would launder a real tampering event just as happily:
,  * The recorded baseline sha256 was ae72c32d405ae8648580bc5f45d6467d, taken
    2026-08-19 19:19:30.
,  * The timestamped backup I made immediately before editing hashes to exactly
    that value. So the baseline IS the pre-edit file, and the drift is precisely
    my change and nothing else.
,  * Structural diff confirms it: 5 nodes added (catch, retry gate, delay,
    api-current-state, rebuild function), 0 modified.
,  * Formatting was not mangled: 11,465 -> 11,578 lines, and both files begin
    identically, so my json.dump(indent=4) matched Node-RED's existing format
    rather than rewriting the whole file.

ONE RED HERRING worth recording. Diffing current against .flows.json.backup also
showed a REMOVED inject node ("TEMP mirror verification"). That was not mine.
.flows.json.backup is the PREVIOUS VERSION, not the approved baseline -- both it
and flows.json carried an 08-19 12:54 mtime, which is a Node-RED UI deploy that
removed the temp node and rotated the old file into the backup. Comparing against
the backup instead of the sha256 baseline would have raised a false alarm about a
change John made legitimately days earlier.

RESOLVED: ran the sanctioned config-drift-rebaseline.service (Result=success).
Baseline now equals the current flows.json, all 7 tracked files read
config_file_drift=0, and the rule went from firing to inactive with 0 active
alerts. Note the config_drift rule group evaluates every 900s, so the alert
lingers up to 15 minutes after the underlying metric clears -- that delay is the
group interval, not a stuck alert.

STANDING NOTE FOR FUTURE FLOW EDITS: any direct edit of flows.json will trip this
alert. That is correct behaviour and should not be suppressed. Verify the diff
against the sha256 baseline (not the .backup), then re-baseline.
#+end_example

#+begin_example
NocoBase was removed 2026-03-14 in f40e2ac01 (alongside n8n, Windows 11 and
ntopng). Restored 2026-08-15 for evaluation, 1216 commits later, against a
configuration that has changed substantially underneath it.

NOTHING of the old deployment survived, so this is a fresh install rather than a
state restore. Verified: no PostgreSQL role or database named nocobase, no
/var/lib/nocobase, no /var/lib/containers/nocobase, no nocobase user or group, no
certificate, no image in any store.

BLOCKED ON SOPS -- the one thing I cannot do. f40e2ac01's own message says "SOPS
secrets must be cleaned up manually", and they were: secrets/secrets.yaml
contains no key matching nocobase (checked by key name only; never decrypted).
sops-nix fails activation for a declared secret whose key is absent, which would
break EVERY rebuild on this host rather than just this service. So the whole
thing is gated behind services.nocobase.enable, which is false.

To enable, John needs to add two keys with `sops /etc/nixos/secrets/secrets.yaml`:

  nocobase-db-password: <password for the PostgreSQL role "nocobase">

  nocobase-secrets: |        # env file, one KEY=VALUE per line
    APP_KEY=<random, openssl rand -hex 32>
    ENCRYPTION_FIELD_KEY=<random, openssl rand -hex 32>
    DB_PASSWORD=<SAME value as nocobase-db-password>
    INIT_ROOT_EMAIL=<first-run admin email>
    INIT_ROOT_PASSWORD=<first-run admin password>

then set services.nocobase.enable = true in hosts/vulcan/default.nix and rebuild.
The certificate provisions itself -- nocobase.vulcan.lan is already in
certs/renew-nginx-certs.sh, which creates as well as renews.

WHAT CHANGED SINCE 2026-03, i.e. why this is not a revert:

,  * DB_HOST is now 10.88.0.1, not 127.0.0.1. The original set POSTGRES_HOST,
    which NocoBase does not read at all (it uses DB_*). 10.88.0.1 is the pinned
    podman bridge address that avoids the 2026-07-03 boot race documented in
    modules/containers/quadlet.nix.
,  * ExecStartPre polls for PostgreSQL instead of a single pg_isready -t 30.
,  * The alert file was NOT restored. 4 of its 7 rules were dead or would be:
    three keyed on systemd_unit_state (0 series here; it is node_systemd_unit_state,
    which sees only SYSTEM units, so a rootless container unit is invisible to it),
    and one watched a mountpoint "/var/lib/nocobase" that is a directory on /, so
    it never had a series. Coverage is now automatic instead: container-health-exporter
    derives its targets from config.home-manager.users, so the generic ContainerDown /
    ContainerUnhealthy / ContainerRestarting / ContainerCPUSaturated /
    ContainerImageOutdated rules apply. Verified by evaluation that "nocobase"
    lands in that derived list. wallabag, the closest analogue, has no per-service
    alert file either.
,  * No timers.nix edit. rootlessContainerUsers is derived now; the hand-maintained
    list that used to drift is gone.
,  * No L+ tmpfiles symlink -- container-users-dedicated.nix explicitly forbids
    them, mandating a sops path= override, which is what this uses.
,  * No Nagios block; Nagios was retired 2026-07-31.
,  * secrets-migration.nix no longer exists; the container-db shared-secret grouping
    went with the shared container users.

The container user is declared in modules/containers/nocobase-quadlet.nix rather
than alongside the others, because it must be gated and a lib.mkIf nested inside
container-users-dedicated.nix's single literal users = { ... } attrset is not a
definition boundary.

VERIFIED both ways by evaluation before any switch: with enable=true the user,
home-manager user, nginx vhost, both sops secrets, container image, blackbox
target, ensureDatabases and ensureUsers entries all materialise; with enable=false
they are all absent.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
DEPLOYED AND SERVING. John added the two SOPS keys 2026-08-16; I enabled services.nocobase.enable, rebuilt, and verified end to end.

Live evidence:
  - container 'nocobase' Up, image docker.io/nocobase/nocobase:latest
  - direct http://127.0.0.1:13000/ -> HTTP 200
  - through nginx https://nocobase.vulcan.lan/ -> HTTP 200 WITH and WITHOUT SNI
    (no-SNI tested deliberately; an SNI-only check once missed 6 vhost breakages)
  - cert served is the one issued: CN=nocobase.vulcan.lan, notAfter Aug 16 2027
  - first-boot migrations ran: 103 tables in the nocobase database
  - secrets land correctly: /run/secrets-nocobase/nocobase-secrets is a symlink
    (mode 777 is normal for a symlink) to a 400 nocobase:nocobase regular file in
    a 750 dir, carrying all five expected keys
  - monitoring is automatic, as designed: container_running{name=nocobase}=1 and
    container_health_status present, because container-health-exporter derives
    its targets from config.home-manager.users and nocobase now appears in that
    derived list

TWO DEFECTS SURFACED AND WERE FIXED ALONG THE WAY:

1. postgresql-nocobase-setup.service failed with 'role "nocobase" does not
   exist', failing switch-to-configuration with exit 4. Cause: mkPostgresUserSetup
   ordered only after postgresql.service, but ensureUsers/ensureDatabases are
   applied by postgresql-setup.service. The race was LATENT FOR EVERY consumer of
   that helper -- they only worked because their roles already existed from an
   earlier boot. Fixed by ordering after/wants postgresql-setup.service; verified
   the deployed unit now lists both.

2. The quadlet generator had not run when the switch reached the user manager, so
   nocobase.service was loaded=bad. A user daemon-reload cleared it and the
   container started. Filed separately as it may recur on boot.

The certificate had to be issued by hand: nocobase.vulcan.lan is in
certs/renew-nginx-certs.sh, but that timer is monthly and next due 2026-09-01, so
nginx would have refused to start referencing a missing cert. Issued with the
documented script at 644/600 nginx:nginx.

** Close Reason

FIXED and verified live in modules/monitoring/alerts/network.yaml.

The expr is avg_over_time(probe_success[24h]) < 0.9, so $value is the SUCCESS rate. The summary rendered it as 'is chronically unreachable (85.86% over 24h)', which parses as 86% DOWN when it actually means 86% UP. For the garage door lock that made a device answering ~87% of pings look like one offline nearly all day. The description immediately below always had it right ('answered only ...'); only the summary was wrong, so this is a pure wording fix with NO change to expr, threshold, severity or dwell.

New text: 'IoT device {{ $labels.instance }} answered only {{ $value | humanizePercentage }} of pings over 24h (below the 90% threshold)'.

VERIFIED after ./build switch: Prometheus /api/v1/rules reports the rule health=ok, lastError=none, carrying the new summary; grep for the old wording returns 0; 485 rules loaded with 29 health=unknown, all with EMPTY lastError, i.e. post-reload warmup rather than breakage.

Found while triaging the firing alert. The firing itself is expected and documented (nixos-mn4); the device decision remains John's and is tracked in nixos-cnc.

#+begin_example
Commit c0187698c ('flake.lock: Update', 2026-08-15 07:49) moved the nix-config input 24b2133cd100 -> aa72a9b0f1e0. That revision's config/obr.nix now gates on BOTH inputs.obr and inputs.rust-overlay:

  hasObrInputs = inputs ? obr && inputs.obr ? outPath
              && inputs ? rust-overlay && inputs.rust-overlay ? outPath;

and packages/source-project-apps.nix does 'pkgs.extend (import inputs.rust-overlay.outPath)' to build obr with a Rust nightly toolchain. /etc/nixos declares obr but not rust-overlay, so the assertion fails and NOTHING can be rebuilt -- not a config change, not a security update.

Proven pre-existing and proven to be this bump, not a local edit:
  - HEAD with no working-tree changes fails the same assertion.
  - Substituting HEAD~1's flake.lock (nix-config 24b2133cd100) evaluates cleanly to
    /nix/store/w79yfvcwvkk1xfqsv8f9jfwysx57n5q5-nixos-system-vulcan-...drv

UPSTREAM DEFECT, worth reporting separately: nix-config requires inputs.rust-overlay but declares it NOWHERE in its own flake.nix (0 occurrences). It is only satisfiable by a consumer that happens to declare it. Fixing it upstream would be the more durable half.
#+end_example

** Close Reason

#+begin_example
FIXED and verified end to end in 779f43fc3.

Root cause confirmed, not inferred: flake.lock update c0187698c (2026-08-15 07:49) moved nix-config 24b2133cd100 -> aa72a9b0f1e0, whose config/obr.nix gates on BOTH inputs.obr and inputs.rust-overlay. Only obr was declared here, so the assertion aborted system.build.toplevel and nothing could be rebuilt. Proven both directions before changing anything: HEAD with a clean working tree failed the identical assertion, and substituting HEAD~1 flake.lock evaluated cleanly to w79yfvcwvkk1xfqsv8f9jfwysx57n5q5.

Fix: declared rust-overlay as a root input, for the same documented reason obr already is -- nix-config is consumed with flake = false, so its outputs function never runs and every input its home-manager modules reference must be supplied by the consumer. inputs.nixpkgs.follows set, so no second nixpkgs was locked.

VERIFIED NOW, this cycle:
  - rust-overlay declared in flake.nix and present as a root input of flake.lock
  - the configuration evaluates to xgkwisqm6rn77a0w6iwm6v9nr7dwdac9
  - nix derivation show proves that drv realises to chjzplqsd2jj6mgmk1ddmlq2nz884di6, which is EXACTLY the running /run/current-system -- so the committed tree is the running system, not merely a thing that once built
  - two full build+switch cycles have completed since, 0 failed units

Still load-bearing: upstream nix-config HEAD is still aa72a9b0f1e0 (git ls-remote, checked this cycle), so no upstream fix has landed and this declaration is what keeps the host buildable.

The 'upstream defect' claim in the original description was WRONG and was corrected in 58b2c8f97 plus a comment on this issue: nix-config obtains rust-overlay legitimately via nix-config-ai lib.inputSet merged as rootInputs // portableInputs, so consumers taking it as a real flake were never affected. The recurring class -- vulcan re-declaring nix-config inputs by hand -- is tracked separately as nixos-czc.
#+end_example

** Comments

    "text": "CORRECTION 2026-08-15, after researching the question rather than leaving it open.\nThe \"UPSTREAM DEFECT\" paragraph in the description above is WRONG and should not\nbe acted on. nix-config is consistent; I had only checked its flake.nix.\n\nWhat I originally claimed: nix-config requires inputs.rust-overlay but declares\nit nowhere in its own flake.nix (0 occurrences), so it is only satisfiable by a\nconsumer that happens to declare it.\n\nWhat is actually true, traced end to end at rev aa72a9b0:\n\n  1. config/ai/flake.nix:12-15 declares rust-overlay, identically to how vulcan\n     now does (url = github:oxalica/rust-overlay, inputs.nixpkgs.follows).\n  2. config/ai/flake.nix:73,83 exports\n       portableInputs = builtins.removeAttrs inputs [ \"self\" ];\n       lib.inputSet   = portableInputs;\n     so rust-overlay is in that set.\n  3. nix-config/flake.nix:141-142 merges it into what its modules see:\n       portableInputs = rootInputs.nix-config-ai.lib.inputSet;\n       inputs         = rootInputs // portableInputs;\n  4. That merged set is handed to home-manager via extraSpecialArgs\n     (flake.nix:199-201, 271), which is what config/obr.nix reads.\n\nSo any consumer taking nix-config as a REAL flake -- hera, darwin -- resolves\ninputs.rust-overlay fine and never sees this break. Upstream has not\nmoved, so no `nix flake update nix-config` is warranted and the consumer-side\ndeclaration in 779f43fc3 remains the load-bearing fix.\n\nOne real consequence, not a defect: vulcan pins rust-overlay ad8ebb59\n(2026-08-15) while nix-config-ai pins 6ef009bf (2026-07-31), so obr is built here\nwith a different toolchain revision than on hera.
